51: def test_corner(sl, sh, dl, dh, wl, wh, kap, N):
52:     sb, db, wb = ball(sl, sh), ball(dl, dh), ball(wl, wh)
53:     N1 = N1f(sb, kap)
54:     Bup = arb((kap * sb * sb / N1).upper())
55:     rp = Bup.sqrt()
56:     xm = (arb(dh) * arb(dh) + arb(wh) * arb(wh)).sqrt()
57:     q1 = rp * xm
58:     if not (q1 < 1 and xm < 1):
59:         return False, "corner-radius"
60:     tail_psi = 2 * rp * rp * q1 ** (N - 1) / (1 - q1) + 3 * xm ** (N - 1) / (1 - xm)
61:     tail_ig = 2 * rp * q1 ** N / (1 - q1) + 3 * xm ** N / (1 - xm)
62:     psi, ig = corner_series_stable(acb(sb), acb(db), acb(wb), kap, N)
63:     psi, ig = psi.real, ig.real
64:     mids = [arb((sl + sh) / 2), arb((dl + dh) / 2), arb((wl + wh) / 2)]
65:     hb = [sym(sl, sh), sym(dl, dh), sym(wl, wh)]
66:     if not (ig - tail_ig >= 0):
67:         def Fi(S, Dl, Om):
68:             return (corner_series_stable(S, Dl, Om, kap, N)[1],)
69:         (igm,) = mvf(Fi, mids, [sb, db, wb], hb)
70:         ig = ig.intersection(igm) if ig.is_finite() else igm
71:         if not (ig - tail_ig >= 0):
72:             return False, "corner-img"
73:     if psi + tail_psi < 0:
74:         return True, "corner"
75: 
76:     def Fp(S, Dl, Om):
77:         return (corner_series_stable(S, Dl, Om, kap, N)[0],)
78:     (pm,) = mvf(Fp, mids, [sb, db, wb], hb)
79:     if pm + tail_psi < 0:
80:         return True, "corner-mvf"
81:     return False, "corner"

SOURCE 18-me3lib_v1.py SHA256 4827676a6b5984941797232c2e36a86f672921276d74de1a73b2f72ac997eaa6 ORIGINAL /Users/jinleic/repos/aifours/mathematics/evidence/six-lane-20261016/me3-minimum-scv/code/me3lib_v1.py
1: """Ball-arithmetic (python-flint arb/acb) primitives for the ME(3) minimum-SCV lane.
2: 
3: PRIMAL (D2 extreme rays, real pole 1, pair -(1+delta) +- i omega, r = delta/omega, phi = omega*tau):
4:   c = (1 - i r) e^{(r - i) phi},  zeta = 1/(1 + r w - i w),  P_k = c zeta^{k+1},  a_k = 1 - Re P_k  (= m_k / k!)
5:   1 + S = 2 a0 a2 / a1^2.
6:   d/dr at fixed (w, phi):  dP_k/dr = P_k [phi + (r - i)/(1 + r^2) - (k+1) w zeta],  a_k' = -Re(dP_k/dr).
7:   S >= T  <=>  G := 2 a0 a2 - (1+T) a1^2 >= 0 (given a1 > 0).
8:   dS/dr > 0  <=>  D := a0' a1 a2 + a0 a1 a2' - 2 a0 a1' a2 > 0 (given a0, a1, a2 > 0).
9: Feasibility: phi <= theta1(r), theta1(r) the root in (pi, 2pi) of g_r(u) = e^{r u}(cos u - r sin u) = 1;
10:   g_r is increasing on (pi, 2pi), so g_r(u) > 1 with u in (pi, 2pi) certifies u > theta1(r).
11: 
12: All functions take arb balls (possibly wide) and return arb balls enclosing every value on the box.
13: """
14: import flint
15: from flint import arb, acb
16: 
17: 
18: def primal_terms(r, w, phi):
19:     """Return (a0, a1, a2, da0, da1, da2) enclosures for arb balls r, w, phi."""
20:     one = acb(1)
21:     c = acb(1, -r) * acb(r * phi, -phi).exp()
22:     zeta = one / acb(1 + r * w, -w)
23:     shift = acb(phi) + acb(r, -1) / (1 + r * r)
24:     a = []
25:     da = []
26:     zp = zeta
27:     for k in range(3):
28:         P = c * zp
29:         a.append(1 - P.real)
30:         dP = P * (shift - (k + 1) * w * zeta)
31:         da.append(-dP.real)
32:         zp = zp * zeta
33:     return a[0], a[1], a[2], da[0], da[1], da[2]
34: 
35: 
36: def primal_G_D(r, w, phi, T):
37:     a0, a1, a2, d0, d1, d2 = primal_terms(r, w, phi)
38:     G = 2 * a0 * a2 - (1 + T) * a1 * a1
39:     D = d0 * a1 * a2 + a0 * a1 * d2 - 2 * a0 * d1 * a2
40:     return a0, a1, a2, G, D
41: 
42: 
43: def g_feas(r, u):
44:     """g_r(u) = e^{r u}(cos u - r sin u)."""
45:     return (r * u).exp() * (u.cos() - r * u.sin())
46: 
47: 
48: def S_point(r, w, phi):
49:     a0, a1, a2, _, _, _ = primal_terms(arb(r), arb(w), arb(phi))
50:     return 2 * a0 * a2 / (a1 * a1) - 1

SOURCE 19-me3jet_v1.py SHA256 0db15d1bf28c6623b978aa61a7ff474dbad089a8028bc4ba856b12fed85c77e6 ORIGINAL /Users/jinleic/repos/aifours/mathematics/evidence/six-lane-20261016/me3-minimum-scv/code/me3jet_v1.py
1: """Forward-mode first-order jets over acb balls, for mean-value-form enclosures.
2: 
3: A jet J(v, d) carries a value enclosure v (acb) and the 3 partial-derivative enclosures d (acb) of a function of
4: 3 real variables over a box. Evaluated with box balls as values and unit vectors as derivatives, every jet operation
5: encloses the true value and gradient over the box (ball arithmetic + the chain rule). Mean-value form:
6:   F(B) is contained in F(m) + sum_j dF_j(B) * [-h_j, h_j]
7: for F continuously differentiable on the box B with midpoint m and half-widths h_j.
8: """
9: from flint import acb, arb
10: 
11: 
12: class J:
13:     __slots__ = ("v", "d")
14: 
15:     def __init__(self, v, d):
16:         self.v = v
17:         self.d = d
18: 
19:     def __add__(a, b):
20:         if isinstance(b, J):
21:             return J(a.v + b.v, (a.d[0] + b.d[0], a.d[1] + b.d[1], a.d[2] + b.d[2]))
22:         return J(a.v + b, a.d)
23: 
24:     __radd__ = __add__
25: 
26:     def __neg__(a):
27:         return J(-a.v, (-a.d[0], -a.d[1], -a.d[2]))
28: 
29:     def __sub__(a, b):
30:         if isinstance(b, J):
31:             return J(a.v - b.v, (a.d[0] - b.d[0], a.d[1] - b.d[1], a.d[2] - b.d[2]))
32:         return J(a.v - b, a.d)
33: 
34:     def __rsub__(a, b):
35:         return J(b - a.v, (-a.d[0], -a.d[1], -a.d[2]))
36: 
37:     def __mul__(a, b):
38:         if isinstance(b, J):
39:             return J(a.v * b.v, (a.v * b.d[0] + b.v * a.d[0], a.v * b.d[1] + b.v * a.d[1], a.v * b.d[2] + b.v * a.d[2]))
40:         return J(a.v * b, (a.d[0] * b, a.d[1] * b, a.d[2] * b))
41: 
42:     __rmul__ = __mul__
43: 
44:     def __truediv__(a, b):
45:         if isinstance(b, J):
46:             q = a.v / b.v
47:             return J(q, ((a.d[0] - q * b.d[0]) / b.v, (a.d[1] - q * b.d[1]) / b.v, (a.d[2] - q * b.d[2]) / b.v))
48:         return J(a.v / b, (a.d[0] / b, a.d[1] / b, a.d[2] / b))
49: 
50:     def __rtruediv__(a, b):
51:         q = b / a.v
52:         f = -q / a.v
53:         return J(q, (f * a.d[0], f * a.d[1], f * a.d[2]))
54: 
55: 
56: def EXP(x):
57:     if isinstance(x, J):
58:         e = x.v.exp()
59:         return J(e, (e * x.d[0], e * x.d[1], e * x.d[2]))
60:     return x.exp()
61: 
62: 
63: def LOG(x):
64:     """Principal log; derivative du/u (branch-independent). Caller handles the branch of the value."""
65:     if isinstance(x, J):
66:         return J(x.v.log(), (x.d[0] / x.v, x.d[1] / x.v, x.d[2] / x.v))
67:     return x.log()
68: 
69: 
70: def RE(x):
71:     if isinstance(x, J):
72:         return J(acb(x.v.real), (acb(x.d[0].real), acb(x.d[1].real), acb(x.d[2].real)))
73:     return acb(x.real)
74: 
75: 
76: def IM(x):
77:     if isinstance(x, J):
78:         return J(acb(x.v.imag), (acb(x.d[0].imag), acb(x.d[1].imag), acb(x.d[2].imag)))
79:     return acb(x.imag)
80: 
81: 
82: def jet_inputs(balls):
83:     z = acb(0)
84:     o = acb(1)
85:     units = [(o, z, z), (z, o, z), (z, z, o)]
86:     return [J(acb(b), units[i]) for i, b in enumerate(balls)]
87: 
88: 
89: def mvf(func, mids, balls, hballs):
90:     """Mean-value-form enclosures of the real outputs of func (a tuple of acb with zero imaginary part).
91:     mids: arb balls containing the midpoints, balls: arb balls containing the box ranges,
92:     hballs: arb balls containing [-h_j, h_j] (built as arb(-h).union(arb(h)) from exact half-widths).
93:     Returns a list of arb enclosures, one per output, valid on the whole box."""
94:     fm = func(*[acb(m) for m in mids])
95:     fj = func(*jet_inputs(balls))
96:     outs = []
97:     for k in range(len(fm)):
98:         e = fm[k].real
99:         for j in range(3):
100:             e = e + fj[k].d[j].real * hballs[j]
101:         outs.append(e)
102:     return outs

SOURCE 20-me3jet_v2.py SHA256 a4f757c30cf964de13431c2526cc2bfba3f11fe9aee2361474992e30608dfc87 ORIGINAL /Users/jinleic/repos/aifours/mathematics/evidence/six-lane-20261016/me3-minimum-scv/code/me3jet_v2.py
1: """First-order jets over acb balls, version 2: identical to me3jet_v1.py plus SQRT (d sqrt(u) = du / (2 sqrt u),
2: principal branch; callers use it only on arguments with positive real part)."""
3: from me3jet_v1 import J, EXP, LOG, RE, IM, jet_inputs, mvf  # noqa: F401
4: 
5: 
6: def SQRT(x):
7:     if isinstance(x, J):
8:         r = x.v.sqrt()
9:         f = 1 / (2 * r)
10:         return J(r, (f * x.d[0], f * x.d[1], f * x.d[2]))
11:     return x.sqrt()

SOURCE 21-cert_primal_v3.py SHA256 b541a8c5984dbf7ee7311804532c4c0edcc77dcbfb0dfc7e76060aa6e266f213 ORIGINAL /Users/jinleic/repos/aifours/mathematics/evidence/six-lane-20261016/me3-minimum-scv/code/cert_primal_v3.py
1: """Certified branch-and-bound on a PRIMAL region (r, w, phi), version 3 (spec JSON in argv[2]).
2: 
3: Same box tests as cert_valley_v2.py (INFEAS | SGE naive-then-MVF | DPOS naive-then-MVF), plus a complete,
4: compressed leaf record for an independent coverage audit:
5:   leaves.txt.gz : one line per leaf, "<cell_index> <path> <OUTCOME>", path over {0,1} (0 = lower half, 1 = upper
6:                   half of the split dimension; the split dimension is a deterministic function of the box, see
7:                   split_dim), in depth-first order. Initial cells are numbered in r-major, then w, then phi order.
8: The leaves of every cell form a complete prefix-free binary code iff they tile the cell (checked by
9: check_cover_v1.py, which also rebuilds exact boxes from the paths).
10: Definitions (real pole 1, pair -(1+delta) +- i w, r = delta/w, phi = w tau):
11:   c = (1 - i r) e^{(r - i) phi}, zeta = 1/(1 + r w - i w), a_k = 1 - Re(c zeta^{k+1}) = m_k/k!,
12:   G = 2 a0 a2 - (1+T) a1^2  (S > T iff G > 0 when a1 > 0),
13:   D = a0' a1 a2 + a0 a1 a2' - 2 a0 a1' a2, a_k' = -Re(c zeta^{k+1} [phi + (r - i)/(1 + r^2) - (k+1) w zeta])
14:       (dS/dr at fixed (w, phi) > 0 iff D > 0 when a0, a1, a2 > 0),
15:   INFEAS: phi_lo > pi, r_lo > 0 and g_{r_lo}(phi_lo) = e^{r_lo phi_lo}(cos phi_lo - r_lo sin phi_lo) > 1.
16: argv[1] = run directory. Writes primal.json and leaves.txt.gz (exclusive create).
17: """
18: import sys
19: import json
20: import time
21: import gzip
22: import hashlib
23: sys.path.insert(0, __file__.rsplit("/", 1)[0])
24: import lane_guard  # noqa: E402
25: import flint  # noqa: E402
26: from flint import arb, acb, fmpq  # noqa: E402
27: import me3lib_v1 as L  # noqa: E402
28: from me3jet_v1 import EXP, RE, mvf  # noqa: E402
29: 
30: RUN = sys.argv[1]
31: spec = json.load(open(sys.argv[2]))
32: flint.ctx.prec = spec["prec"]
33: 
34: 
35: def Q(s):
36:     s = str(s)
37:     if "/" in s:
38:         a, b = s.split("/")
39:         return fmpq(int(a), int(b))
40:     return fmpq(int(s))
41: 
42: 
43: T = Q(spec["T"])
44: Tb = arb(T)
45: reg = [(Q(spec["region"][k][0]), Q(spec["region"][k][1])) for k in ("r", "w", "phi")]
46: nsplit = [spec["initial_split"][k] for k in ("r", "w", "phi")]
47: PI = arb.pi()
48: I = acb(0, 1)
49: maxdepth = spec["max_depth"]
50: 
51: 
52: def primal_GD_generic(R, Wv, Ph):
53:     c = (1 - I * R) * EXP((R - I) * Ph)
54:     zeta = 1 / (1 + R * Wv - I * Wv)
55:     shift = Ph + (R - I) / (1 + R * R)
56:     a, da = [], []
57:     zp = zeta
58:     for k in range(3):
59:         P = c * zp
60:         a.append(1 - RE(P))
61:         dP = P * (shift - (k + 1) * Wv * zeta)
62:         da.append(-RE(dP))
63:         zp = zp * zeta
64:     G = 2 * a[0] * a[2] - (1 + Tb) * a[1] * a[1]
65:     D = da[0] * a[1] * a[2] + a[0] * a[1] * da[2] - 2 * a[0] * da[1] * a[2]
66:     return (G, D)
67: 
68: 
69: def ball(lo, hi):
70:     return arb(lo).union(arb(hi))
71: 
72: 
73: stats = {"naive_SGE": 0, "mvf_SGE": 0, "naive_DPOS": 0, "mvf_DPOS": 0}
74: 
75: 
76: def classify(box):
77:     (rl, rh), (wl, wh), (pl, ph) = box
78:     if arb(pl) > PI and rl > 0:
79:         if L.g_feas(arb(rl), arb(pl)) > 1:
80:             return "INFEAS"
81:     rb, wb, pb = ball(rl, rh), ball(wl, wh), ball(pl, ph)
82:     a0, a1, a2, G, D = L.primal_G_D(rb, wb, pb, Tb)
83:     apos = a0 > 0 and a1 > 0 and a2 > 0
84:     if a1 > 0 and G > 0:
85:         stats["naive_SGE"] += 1
86:         return "SGE"
87:     if apos and D > 0:
88:         stats["naive_DPOS"] += 1
89:         return "DPOS"
90:     mids = [arb((rl + rh) / 2), arb((wl + wh) / 2), arb((pl + ph) / 2)]
91:     hb = [ball(-(rh - rl) / 2, (rh - rl) / 2), ball(-(wh - wl) / 2, (wh - wl) / 2), ball(-(ph - pl) / 2, (ph - pl) / 2)]
92:     Gm, Dm = mvf(primal_GD_generic, mids, [rb, wb, pb], hb)
93:     if a1 > 0 and Gm > 0:
94:         stats["mvf_SGE"] += 1
95:         return "SGE"
96:     if apos and Dm > 0:
97:         stats["mvf_DPOS"] += 1
98:         return "DPOS"
99:     return None
100: 
101: 
102: def split_dim(box):
103:     """Deterministic: largest width scaled by spec weights; ties -> lowest index."""
104:     wts = spec["split_weights"]
105:     sc = [(box[i][1] - box[i][0]) * Q(wts[i]) for i in range(3)]
106:     best = 0
107:     for i in (1, 2):
108:         if sc[i] > sc[best]:
109:             best = i
110:     return best
111: 
112: 
113: def children(box):
114:     i = split_dim(box)
115:     lo, hi = box[i]
116:     mid = (lo + hi) / 2
117:     b0, b1 = list(box), list(box)
118:     b0[i], b1[i] = (lo, mid), (mid, hi)
119:     return tuple(b0), tuple(b1)
120: 
121: 
122: t0 = time.time()
123: cells = []
124: for i in range(nsplit[0]):
125:     for j in range(nsplit[1]):
126:         for k in range(nsplit[2]):
127:             idx = (i, j, k)
128:             cells.append(tuple((reg[d][0] + (reg[d][1] - reg[d][0]) * idx[d] / nsplit[d],
129:                                 reg[d][0] + (reg[d][1] - reg[d][0]) * (idx[d] + 1) / nsplit[d]) for d in range(3)))
130: 
131: counts = {"INFEAS": 0, "SGE": 0, "DPOS": 0, "FAIL": 0}
132: depth_hist = {}
133: fails = []
134: hasher = hashlib.sha256()
135: dpos_bb = None
136: evals = 0
137: gz = gzip.GzipFile(fileobj=lane_guard.xopen(RUN + "/leaves.txt.gz", "xb"), mode="wb", mtime=0)
138: capped = False
139: for ci, cell in enumerate(cells):
140:     stack = [(cell, "")]
141:     while stack:
142:         box, path = stack.pop()
143:         out = classify(box)
144:         evals += 1
145:         if out is None:
146:             if len(path) >= maxdepth:
147:                 out = "FAIL"
148:                 if len(fails) < 50:
149:                     fails.append({"cell": ci, "path": path, "box": [[str(x) for x in iv] for iv in box]})
150:             else:
151:                 b0, b1 = children(box)
152:                 stack.append((b1, path + "1"))
153:                 stack.append((b0, path + "0"))
154:                 continue
155:         counts[out] += 1
156:         depth_hist[len(path)] = depth_hist.get(len(path), 0) + 1
157:         line = f"{ci} {path if path else '-'} {out}\n"
158:         gz.write(line.encode())
159:         hasher.update(line.encode())
160:         if out == "DPOS":
161:             lo = [float(iv[0]) for iv in box]
162:             hi = [float(iv[1]) for iv in box]
163:             dpos_bb = [lo, hi] if dpos_bb is None else [[min(a, b) for a, b in zip(dpos_bb[0], lo)],
164:                                                          [max(a, b) for a, b in zip(dpos_bb[1], hi)]]
165:         if evals % 200000 == 0:
166:             print("progress", evals, counts, stats, round(time.time() - t0, 1), flush=True)
167:         if evals > spec["eval_cap"]:
168:             capped = True
169:             break
170:     if capped:
171:         print("EVAL CAP REACHED; stopping without conclusion", flush=True)
172:         break
173: gz.close()
174: summary = {"spec": spec["name"], "counts": counts, "stats": stats, "evaluations": evals, "cells": len(cells),
175:            "eval_cap_hit": capped, "depth_histogram": {str(k): v for k, v in sorted(depth_hist.items())},
176:            "fails_first50": fails, "dpos_bounding_box_float": dpos_bb, "leaf_lines_sha256": hasher.hexdigest(),
177:            "certificate_ok": counts["FAIL"] == 0 and not capped, "elapsed_seconds": round(time.time() - t0, 2)}
178: with lane_guard.xopen(RUN + "/primal.json") as fh:
179:     json.dump(summary, fh, indent=1)
180: print("SUMMARY", json.dumps(summary))

143: * 14 `import math`; 51 `0.5`; 52 `math.cos/sin/exp`; 68 `float(…)/float(…)`; 70 `math.floor(th*10000)` and `1e-6`:
144:   **OK-heuristic**.
145:   * They are used only for kind G, to pick the TRIANGLE angle θ′ = fmpq(⌊10⁴·th⌋, 10⁴).
146:   * Since `theta1_float` starts its bisection at lo = π and only raises lo, th ≥ π, so θ′ ≥ 31415/10⁴ > 0.
147:   * TRIANGLE validity needs only θ′ ≥ 0; the review concurs. θ′ then enters as an exact rational ball.
148:   * For SWC (kind C) this code is not executed.
149: * 123 split midpoint `(lo + hi)/2` (fmpq/int): OK-exact.
150: * 134, 145 `round(time…)`: OK-reporting.

152:   floats.

154: ### Coverage checkers
155: * `check_cover_v1.py` 40, 41, 58 and `check_cover_v2.py` 47: Fraction arithmetic. OK-exact.
156: * `check_cover_v3.py` 92 `arb.pi() / 2` (arb/int) and 123 (Fraction): OK-exact.
157: * The skip-d rule uses arb balls: 53-bit default precision in v2 and 128 bits in v3. A True comparison is certified
158:   in either case.
159: 
160: ### `lane_guard.py` and `run_v1.sh`
161: * `lane_guard.py` contains no arithmetic. On import it replaces `os.remove`, `os.unlink`, `os.rmdir`,
162:   `os.removedirs`, `os.replace`, `os.rename`, `shutil.rmtree`, `shutil.move` and the `tempfile` creators with a
163:   raising stub. `xopen` is `open(path, 'x'|'xb')` and `new_dir` is `os.makedirs(exist_ok=False)`. It has no effect
164:   on numerics.
165: * `run_v1.sh` does integer disk-gate arithmetic and float wall time (OK-reporting) only.
166: 
167: ## Acceptance logic (non-float, for completeness)
168: 
169: * Every acceptance is a positive certified ball comparison: `<`, `>`, `>=` or `<=`, all of which are False when the
170:   balls overlap or are non-finite (smoke B5–B6).
171: * Negated comparisons occur only on rejection or fallback paths:
172:   * `if not (…): return False`;
173:   * `_ld_ratio_small_beta` returning None;
174:   * the corner `corner-img` rejection.
175: * Intersections are guarded by `is_finite()`. The SWB naive and MVF paths require finite B, C, M₄ and M₅.
176: * So an indeterminate evaluation can only lead to a split or a FAIL, never to a CERT, SGE, DPOS, INFEAS or SKIP.

78: 
79: **Method.**
80: 1. I read every line by hand and traced the call graph from each driver (section 2).
81: 2. An AST scanner I wrote (`independent-float-audit/code/scan_sites_v1.py`) lists sites by syntax. It ran once
82:    through the lane launcher as run `s01-scan`; receipts are in `independent-float-audit/receipts/s01-scan-*.json`.
83:    It found 1040 sites. I classified every site of these kinds: `/`, `//`, `%`, `**`, float literals, `math.*`,
84:    float(), round(), int(), str(), Fraction(), fmpq(), arb()/acb() constructors, abs(), bool(), `.upper()`,
85:    time.time(), min/max, divmod, json load/dump, default arguments, and comparisons with float operands.
86:    That gives 556 sites. Per-file totals equal the scanner's per-kind counts.
87: 3. Remaining scanner rows were checked but are not tabulated: other comparisons (section 5 covers the acceptance
88:    ones), imports, len/range/open, and transcendental ball methods.
89: 4. The scanner found no `//`, no negative or float exponents, no augmented `/=`, and no float constants in
90:    comparisons. It found no numpy, mpmath or decimal imports; `math` is imported only by cert_dual_v5/v7.
91: 
92: **Semantics assumed** (given trusted facts, plus standard Arb/Python facts; not executed by me):
93: * Python ints convert exactly; Python floats convert as their binary value.
94: * fmpq arithmetic is exact, including fmpq/int and int/fmpq.
95: * arb/acb arithmetic is outward-rounded. `arb(fmpq)` and arb-op-fmpq give enclosures (arb_fmpz_div_fmpz).
96: * Ball comparisons are True only when certain. `arb.upper()` returns an arb, not a float.
97: * `fractions.Fraction` arithmetic is exact, and Fraction/int stays a Fraction.
98: * In Python 3.14, int/int returns a float.
99: * json parses numbers containing `.`, `e`/`E`, NaN or Infinity as float, and every other number as int.
100: 
101: Type keys used in the tables: Q = fmpq, I = Python int, F = Python float, R = arb, C = acb, J = me3jet_v1.J jet
102: (acb components; real-arb components for me3dual_v6._rjets2), Fr = Fraction, S = str.
103:

134: ## 3. Spec numbers (JSON) and how they are parsed
135: 
136: The scanner walked every JSON leaf.
137: 
138: | spec | leaves | float leaves | ints (path = value) | duplicate keys |
139: |---|---|---|---|---|
140: | spec_primal_P_v1.json | 25 | **0** | prec=128, initial_split r/w/phi = 12/80/64, max_depth=40, eval_cap=40000000 | none |
141: | spec_dual_v1.json | 241 | **0** | prec=128, SWC.series_terms=20, max_depth 28/28/32/24/26/26, eval_cap per chart | none |
142: | spec_dual_v3.json | 34 | **0** | prec=128, SWC.series_terms=20, max_depth=28, eval_cap=40000000 | none |
143: 
144: * All rationals are strings of the form "int" or "int/int". Examples: T = "11/50", the region ends, breakpoints,
145:   split weights, and params "1/5", "40", "3", "15708/10000", "1/16", "1/65".
146: * The only other strings are names, kinds and prose.
147: * Drivers parse rationals with Q(), which is str, then int, then fmpq. A JSON float would make `int()` raise
148:   ValueError, so the drivers fail closed.
149: * The checkers parse with `Fraction(str(x))`, which gives the same value for every string present.
150: * The ints are used only for these purposes, never as rational data: precision, the split counts (exact Q/I and
151:   Fr/I divisors), depth and evaluation caps, and N.
152: 
153: ## 4. Site tables (every site; L:C = line:col)
154: 
155: Reach keys:
156: * acc:X = certified acceptance decision in chart X.
157: * geom = box geometry in a driver; geom-audit = geometry replay in a checker.
158: * heur:G = TRIANGLE candidate selection in d01-G.
159: * report = logs or summary JSON only.
160: * none = not executed in any listed certified run.
161:

252: 
253: Why OK-heuristic is valid here:
254: * theta_tri is either None or the exact rational floor(10^4 th)/10^4, with th >= pi. So theta_tri >= 31415/10000,
255:   which is > 0.
256: * It enters only `me3dual_v4.test_general:84-92` as `arb(theta_tri)`.
257: * The TRIANGLE certificate W = a' + b' e^{-xi t'} with a', b' >= 0, a' + b' <= 1 and t' = theta'/w needs only
258:   t' >= 0 (me3-theorem-v3.md:79). Both 0 = 0·e^0 and 1 = e^{-xi·0} lie in the hull.
259: * The ball tests at v4:88-91 then decide acceptance rigorously.
260: * A bad float choice can only make TRIANGLE fail.

427: ## 5. Live acceptance decisions vs indeterminate balls
428: 
429: Every live acceptance is decided in one of two ways:
430: * A ball comparison that is True only when certain. Then a NaN or infinite ball can only lead to reject or split.
431: * An exact Q or Fraction comparison.
432: 
433: The decisions checked:
434: * cert_primal_v3: 78-79 (INFEAS), 83-84 and 87 (naive SGE/DPOS), 93 and 96 (MVF).
435: * cert_dual_v5/v7:66 (SKIP:in-primal-P, exact Q).
436: * v4: 55-59 (branch selection; all three need certainty), 67 (SMALL), 70 and 73 (RADIAL), 77 and 82 (RADIAL-MVF),
437:   88 and 91 (TRIANGLE).
438: * v3: 118 (LW SMALL); 127, 131 and 133 (small-beta ratio, all inside `if not (...)` guards).
439: * v6: 35-41 (argument candidates need certain signs), 48-59, 77 and 79 (skips).
440: * v6.test_bedge: 120, 130, 138, 142-144 and 152-155.
441: * v9: 60, 68, 73, 75 and 81.
442: * check_cover_v2: 59, 62 and 65.
443: 
444: The one pattern where a False comparison is permissive is `Cp = arb(C.upper()) if C.upper() > 0 else arb(0)`, at
445: v6:143 and v6:154. In the live v6 it runs only after `Cv.is_finite()` (142) and `Cm.is_finite()` (152) are True.
446: Then `C.upper()` is a finite exact point, the comparison is decided exactly, and Cp = 0 means sup C <= 0.
447: 
448: Intersections at v4:49-50, v6:135-136 and v9:72 combine two valid enclosures. An empty intersection would raise,
449: which fails closed.
450: 
451: ## 6. Non-float observations (separate from the counts)
452: 
453: 1. **DEAD `me3dual_v3.test_bedge` lines 309-311 and 319-320.** There is no finiteness guard. If Cv or Cm is NaN,
454:    then `arb(0).union(Cv)` and `.upper()` give NaN, `NaN > 0` is False, and Cp silently becomes 0. The acceptance
455:    `Bv + wh^2 Cp + E < 0` could then pass with C unknown. A NaN g3 is caught first by imlow at 304-306. A NaN g2
456:    alone, or a NaN in the MVF Cm, is not caught. [INFERENCE: this relies on Arb's NaN propagation in union and
457:    upper bound; I did not execute it.] It is fixed in the live v6 (142, 152). It ran only in calibration k02.
458: 2. **Spec hashes are not in the launcher receipts.** run_v1.sh hashes `code/*.py` and `code/*.sh`, not the spec
459:    JSONs, so spec provenance relies on the separate freeze receipts cited in me3-theorem-v3.md:95-119. I did not
460:    check those receipts. The spec_primal and spec_dual_v1 hashes above match the prefixes quoted there.
461:    spec_dual_v3 (43d2e22e…d936) is not cited by theorem v3.
462: 3. **me3-theorem-v3.md still lists d02-SWC as the SWC certificate.** Its table (line 135) and text (line 126)
463:    predate d03-SWC and cite the v8 path. The final statement must cite d03-SWC and its coverage audit (b03 used
464:    check_cover_v3.py, which is out of my scope).
465: 4. **The checkers and drivers parse spec values differently.** The checkers use `Fraction(str(x))`; the drivers
466:    use Q(), which accepts only "int" or "int/int". They agree on every value present. If a float were introduced,
467:    the drivers would fail closed, but check_cover_v1:40-41 would silently compute float cell bounds if
468:    `initial_split` were a JSON float. Today all three are ints.
469: 5. **check_cover_v1 does not hard-code "no FAIL".** It enforces only the allowed-outcome list from argv; a01
470:    passed `INFEAS,SGE,DPOS`, so FAIL was excluded (receipt a01-coverP-pre.json).
471: 6. **Checked and found fine:**
472:    * Library modules create `acb(0, 1)` at import, before `flint.ctx.prec` is set. That value is exact, and pi is
473:      computed at call time.
474:    * LD β boxes reaching past pi/2 (15708/10000) and primal φ up to 31416/5000 contain only infeasible points
475:      there. A tan pole gives a non-finite ratio and the box is rejected or falls back to the safe ratio.
476:    * SKIP rules use exact rationals (in-primal-P, and skip-s in the checker) or certain-only ball comparisons
477:      (skip-d, and skip-s in the driver).
478:    * The B-edge test ignores w_lo, which is safe because it certifies all of (0, wh].
479: 7. **run_v1.sh has no numeric influence on the certified process.**
480:    * Float or integer arithmetic appears only in the disk gate (lines 16-18, integer), timing (51, 57, 68:
481:      `round(T1-T0, 3)`, report) and the receipt version probe (35, separate process).
482:    * `ulimit -t` and `/usr/bin/time -l` wrap the run.
483:    * The interpreter is run with `-B` only; no environment variable touches flint precision.
484:    * TMPDIR is the lane tmp directory.
485: 
486: ## 7. Reproduction
487: 
488: `cd L && ./code/run_v1.sh independent-float-audit s01-scan 120 1000000 independent-float-audit/code/scan_sites_v1.py`
489: wrote `runs/s01-scan/{sites.tsv,json_types.tsv,summary.json,stdout.txt}`, exit 0. The scanner reads lane files as
490: text and imports nothing from `code/`. The 556-site tables above were transcribed from `sites.tsv` and classified
491: by hand. Per-file site totals equal the scanner's per-kind counts.

42: 
43: ## 0. Conventions and imports
44: 
45: **0.1 Exponential polynomials.** An *exponential polynomial* (e.p.) is f(t) = Σ_{λ∈Λ} p_λ(t) e^{λt} with Λ ⊂ ℂ
46: finite and p_λ ∈ ℂ[t]∖{0}. Λ is the set of *contributing exponents*, ord f := Σ_λ (deg p_λ + 1) is the *order*, and
47: E(f) := {λ with multiplicity deg p_λ + 1} is the *exponent multiset*. By (I1) the representation is unique. Hence a
48: real-valued e.p. has Λ = Λ̄ and p_{λ̄} = conj(p_λ).
49: 
50: **0.2 Moments.** Densities live on [0, ∞). Take a measurable f ≥ 0 on [0, ∞) with **0 < ∫_0^∞ f** and
51: ∫_0^∞ (1 + t²) f < ∞ (positive mass, i.e. f ≠ 0 in L¹; pointwise f ≢ 0 is NOT enough, e.g. f = 𝟙_{{0}}), and put
52: m_k := ∫_0^∞ t^k f(t) dt (k = 0, 1, 2), SCV(f) := m0 m2 / m1² − 1, and S(f) := SCV/(1 + SCV) = 1 − m1²/(m0 m2).
53: Here m0 > 0, and m1 > 0 because {f > 0} has positive measure while {0} is null. SCV ≥ 0 by Cauchy–Schwarz
54: (m1² ≤ m0 m2). An e.p. defined on ℝ is restricted to [0, ∞) for moments.
55: 
56: **0.3 Imports (ordinary background only).**
57: * (I1) The functions t^k e^{λt} (distinct pairs (k, λ)) are linearly independent over ℂ on every interval of positive
58:   length. A nonzero e.p. is an entire function, so its real zeros are isolated and have finite multiplicity.
59: * (I2) Jordan/spectral form of e^{At}; Cayley–Hamilton; existence and uniqueness for linear constant-coefficient ODE
60:   initial value problems; companion matrices.
61: * (I3) Rolle's theorem.
62: * (I4) Hermite–Genocchi formula, including coincident (confluent) nodes. For ϕ ∈ C²(ℝ) and nodes x1 ≤ x2 ≤ x3,
63:   ϕ[x1,x2,x3] = ∫_Σ ϕ''(u1x1 + u2x2 + u3x3) du2 du3, where Σ := {(u2,u3) : u2, u3 ≥ 0, u2 + u3 ≤ 1} and
64:   u1 := 1 − u2 − u3. The (confluent) divided difference ϕ[x1,x2,x3] is a linear combination, with coefficients
65:   depending only on the nodes, of the values ϕ^{(j)}(x_i), j < multiplicity of x_i.
66: * (I5) Minkowski's theorem (finite-dimensional Krein–Milman) with Carathéodory: every point of a compact convex set
67:   in ℝ^n is a finite convex combination of extreme points.
68: * (I6) Extreme value theorem, intermediate value theorem, Tonelli's theorem.
69: * (I7) Implicit function theorem (C¹).
70: * (I8) For ν ∈ ℝ∖{0}, (1/L)∫_0^L e^{iνt} dt → 0 as L → ∞.
71: 
72: ---
73: 
74: ## 1. L1 — structure of ME(≤3)
75: 
76: **Lemma 1.1 (decay).** If an e.p. G satisfies G(t) → 0 as t → +∞, then every contributing exponent of G has negative
77: real part.
78: 
79: *Proof.* Let G ≢ 0, σ := max_{λ∈Λ} Re λ, d := max{deg p_λ : Re λ = σ}, Λ* := {λ : Re λ = σ, deg p_λ = d}, and let
80: c_λ ≠ 0 be the leading coefficient of p_λ. For t ≥ 1, t^{−d} e^{−σt} G(t) = T(t) + ε(t), where
81: T(t) := Σ_{λ∈Λ*} c_λ e^{i(Im λ)t} and ε(t) → 0. Each remaining term has either a lower polynomial degree or a smaller
82: real part. The numbers Im λ (λ ∈ Λ*) are distinct, so by (I8)
83: (1/L)∫_0^L |T|² = Σ|c_λ|² + Σ_{λ≠λ'} c_λ conj(c_{λ'}) (1/L)∫_0^L e^{i(Im λ − Im λ')t} dt → Σ|c_λ|² > 0.
84: If σ ≥ 0, then |t^{−d} e^{−σt} G(t)| ≤ |G(t)| → 0 for t ≥ 1. This forces T(t) → 0, hence (1/L)∫_0^L |T|² → 0, a
85: contradiction. So σ < 0. ∎
86: 
87: **Lemma 1.2 (dominant real exponent).** Let f ≢ 0 be a real-valued e.p. with f(t) ≥ 0 for all t ≥ t0. Then
88: σ := max Re λ over the contributing exponents is itself a contributing exponent (in particular real). Consequently, a
89: contributing non-real λ forces a contributing real λ0 ≥ Re λ. The case λ0 = Re λ is allowed: it is D1.
90: 
91: *Proof.* Suppose σ ∉ Λ. Define d, Λ*, c_λ, T, ε as in 1.1. Every λ ∈ Λ* is now non-real and Λ* = conj(Λ*), so T is
92: real-valued. T has no zero frequency, so the mean M(T) := lim (1/L)∫_0^L T is 0. Also M(T²) = Σ_{Λ*} |c_λ|² > 0.
93: For t ≥ max(t0, 1) we have t^{−d} e^{−σt} f(t) ≥ 0, so T ≥ −ε and T⁻ := max(−T, 0) ≤ |ε| → 0. Hence M(T⁻) = 0. Then
94: |T| = T + 2T⁻ gives M(|T|) = 0, and T² ≤ ‖T‖_∞ |T| gives M(T²) = 0, a contradiction. ∎
95: 
96: **Theorem L1(a) (structure).** Let X be continuous with an ME representation (α, A) of order n ≤ 3 (HSTZ Def. 1–2):
97: F(t) = Pr(X < t) = 1 − α e^{At} 𝟙 for t ≥ 0, with α real 1×n and A real n×n. Put G(t) := α e^{At} 𝟙 and
98: f(t) := −α A e^{At} 𝟙. Then:
99: 1. α𝟙 = 1, and G(t) = Pr(X ≥ t) for t ≥ 0.
100: 2. G is a real e.p. with ord G ≤ n. Its contributing exponents are eigenvalues of A, all with Re < 0.
101: 3. f = −G' is a density of X: f ≥ 0 on [0, ∞), ∫_0^∞ f = 1, E(f) = E(G), ord f ≤ 3, all moments finite.
102: 4. If f has a contributing non-real exponent, then E(f) = {λ0, λ, λ̄} with λ0 real, λ0 ≥ Re λ, all simple.
103: 
104: *Proof.* (1) F(0) = Pr(X < 0) = 0 gives α𝟙 = 1, and G = 1 − F.
105: (2) By (I2), e^{At} = Σ_{λ∈spec A} e^{λt} Σ_{k<N_λ} (t^k/k!)(A − λ)^k P_λ, where P_λ are the spectral projectors and
106: N_λ the algebraic multiplicities. So G = Σ e^{λt} q_λ with deg q_λ ≤ N_λ − 1, and dropping the zero q_λ gives
107: ord G ≤ Σ N_λ = n. G(t) = Pr(X ≥ t) → 0 (X is a.s. finite), so Lemma 1.1 applies.
108: (3) d/dt e^{At} = A e^{At}, so f = −G'. F = 1 − G is C¹ on [0, ∞) and nondecreasing with F(0) = 0. So
109: F(t) = ∫_0^t f, f = F' ≥ 0 (one-sided at 0), and ∫_0^∞ f = lim F = 1. Every contributing λ satisfies λ ≠ 0, and
110: (q e^{λt})' = (q' + λq) e^{λt} with deg(q' + λq) = deg q; hence E(f) = E(G). Moments are finite by exponential decay.
111: (4) Lemma 1.2 gives a real contributing λ0 ≥ Re λ. λ̄ also contributes because f is real, so ord f ≤ 3 forces
112: E(f) = {λ0, λ, λ̄} with all multiplicities 1. ∎
113: 
114: **Theorem L1(b) (converse).** Let f be a real e.p. whose contributing exponents all have Re < 0, with ord f = N ≤ 3,
115: f ≥ 0 on [0, ∞) and ∫_0^∞ f = 1. Then f is the density of a continuous X ∈ ME(N) ⊆ ME(3), with an explicit real
116: representation of order N, and f = −α A e^{At} 𝟙.
117: 
118: *Proof.* Put G(t) := ∫_t^∞ f. For Re λ < 0, ∫_t^∞ p(s) e^{λs} ds = q(t) e^{λt} with
119: q = −Σ_{j=0}^{deg p} (−1)^j λ^{−j−1} p^{(j)}, and deg q = deg p. So G is a real e.p. with E(G) = E(f) and G(0) = 1.
120: Let χ(s) := Π_{λ∈Λ} (s − λ)^{m_λ} with m_λ := deg p_λ + 1. χ is real, monic and of degree N, and χ(D)G = 0. Put
121: x(t) := (G, G', …, G^{(N−1)})ᵀ. Then x' = Cx with C the companion matrix of χ, so
122: G(t) = e1ᵀ e^{Ct} x(0) = x(0)ᵀ e^{Cᵀt} e1. Let S := [𝟙, e2, …, eN] (det S = 1, S e1 = 𝟙), A := S Cᵀ S^{−1} and
123: α := x(0)ᵀ S^{−1}. Then α e^{At} 𝟙 = x(0)ᵀ e^{Cᵀt} S^{−1} 𝟙 = x(0)ᵀ e^{Cᵀt} e1 = G(t). If X has density f, then
124: Pr(X < t) = 1 − G(t) = 1 − α e^{At} 𝟙 for t ≥ 0, and −α A e^{At} 𝟙 = −G' = f. ∎
125: *Control:* run `ctl-a-v1` (a4) checks this construction exactly for f = 2e^{−t}(1 − cos t). It gives
126: A = [[2,0,−2],[7,0,−6],[4,1,−5]] and α = (1,0,0), and verifies α𝟙 = 1, charpoly(A) = (s+1)(s²+2s+2),
127: αA^k𝟙 = G^{(k)}(0) and −αA^{k+1}𝟙 = f^{(k)}(0) for k = 0, 1, 2. ODE uniqueness then gives αe^{At}𝟙 = G.
128: 
129: **Corollary 1.3.** For ω > 0 and φ ∈ ℝ, e^{−t}(1 − cos(ωt − φ)), normalized, is the density of a continuous
130: X ∈ ME(3): it has exponents −1 and −1 ± iω, it is ≥ 0, and m0 > 0 because it is continuous, ≥ 0 and ≢ 0. Erlang-3:
131: α = (1,0,0) and the bidiagonal A = −I + N give f = t²e^{−t}/2 and SCV = 1/3 exactly (`ctl-a-v1` (a1), exact sympy;
132: E(t) = e^{−t}(I + Nt + N²t²/2) is verified to satisfy E(0) = I and E' = AE).
133: 
134: **Lemma 1.4 (invariance).** SCV(cf) = SCV(f) for c > 0. Also SCV(f(c·)) = SCV(f), because m_k(f(c·)) = c^{−k−1} m_k(f).
135: The time change t ↦ t/μ sends an exponent λ to λ/μ.
136: 
137: **Normal forms.** By L1(a)(4) and Lemma 1.4, the density of every continuous X ∈ ME(≤3) is of one of two types.
138: * (R) All contributing exponents are real, and E(f) is a real multiset of size ≤ 3.
139: * (D) E(f) = {−μ, −μ' ± iν} with μ > 0, ν > 0, μ' ≥ μ. Then g(t) := f(t/μ) has E(g) = {−1, −(1 + δ) ± iω} with
140:   δ := μ'/μ − 1 ≥ 0 and ω := ν/μ > 0, and SCV(g) = SCV(f). **D1:** δ = 0. **D2:** δ > 0.
141: 
142: ---
143: 
144: ## 2. L2 — cones, ratio form, mixtures, extreme rays, bookkeeping
145: 
146: **Definitions.** An *admissible* multiset E is a conjugation-closed multiset in {Re < 0} with |E| ≤ 3. V_E is the real
147: vector space of real e.p. whose exponent multiset is contained in E, together with 0. It has dim V_E = |E|, with basis
148: t^k e^{λt} (λ real, k < mult λ) and e^{(Re λ)t} cos((Im λ)t), e^{(Re λ)t} sin((Im λ)t) for a non-real pair (whose
149: multiplicity is 1 when |E| ≤ 3). K_E := {f ∈ V_E : f(t) ≥ 0 ∀ t ≥ 0} and B_E := {f ∈ K_E : ∫_0^∞ f = 1}.
150: By L1(a)–(b), the densities of continuous ME(≤3) laws are exactly ∪_E B_E over admissible E.
151: 
152: **Lemma 2.1 (cone).** K_E is a closed, convex, pointed cone. ℓ(f) := ∫_0^∞ f and m1(f) are > 0 on K_E∖{0}, and B_E is
153: compact and convex.
154: 
155: *Proof.* Each evaluation f ↦ f(t) is a linear functional on the finite-dimensional V_E, so K_E is an intersection of
156: closed half-spaces. If ±f ∈ K_E then f = 0 on [0, ∞), so f = 0 by (I1). For f ∈ K_E∖{0}, f ≥ 0 is continuous and
157: ≢ 0 on [0, ∞) by (I1), so ℓ(f) > 0 and m1(f) > 0. Fix a norm. ℓ is continuous and positive on the compact set
158: K_E ∩ {‖f‖ = 1}, so ℓ ≥ c > 0 there. Hence B_E ⊆ {‖f‖ ≤ 1/c}, which is closed and bounded. ∎
159: 
160: **Lemma 2.2 (ratio form).** For f as in 0.2, S(f) = min_{θ>0} R_θ(f), where
161: R_θ(f) := ∫_0^∞ (t − θ)² f / (θ² ∫_0^∞ f). The minimum is attained at θ = m2/m1.
162: 
163: *Proof.* With x := 1/θ > 0, R_θ = 1 − 2(m1/m0)x + (m2/m0)x². This is minimized at x = m1/m2 > 0, with value
164: 1 − m1²/(m0 m2) = S(f). ∎
165: 
166: **Lemma 2.3 (mixtures).** Let ν be a finite positive measure on M with ν(M) > 0, and g : M × [0, ∞) → [0, ∞)
167: measurable. Assume each g_μ := g(μ, ·) satisfies 0.2 (in particular 0 < ∫ g_μ), ∫_M ∫_0^∞ (1 + t²) g_μ dt dν < ∞,
168: and SCV(g_μ) ≥ c ≥ 0 for ν-a.e. μ. Then f := ∫_M g_μ dν(μ) satisfies 0.2 and SCV(f) ≥ c. In particular,
169: SCV(f1 + … + fk) ≥ min_i SCV(f_i) whenever every f_i satisfies 0.2 (positive mass).
170: 
171: *Proof.* x ↦ x/(1 + x) is increasing, so S(g_μ) ≥ c' := c/(1 + c). Fix θ > 0. By 2.2,
172: ∫(t − θ)² g_μ ≥ c' θ² ∫ g_μ for a.e. μ. Integrate over ν (Tonelli) to get ∫(t − θ)² f ≥ c' θ² ∫ f, i.e. R_θ(f) ≥ c'.
173: f satisfies 0.2: ∫ f = ∫_M (∫ g_μ) dν > 0, because the inner integral is > 0 for every μ and ν(M) > 0; and
174: ∫(1 + t²) f < ∞ by hypothesis and Tonelli. Minimizing over θ gives S(f) ≥ c', i.e. SCV(f) ≥ c. ∎
175: 
176: **Remark 2.3a (every application satisfies 0.2).**
177: 1. Every f ∈ K_E∖{0} is a nonnegative continuous e.p. whose exponents have negative real part. So ∫(1 + t²) f < ∞,
178:    and ∫ f > 0 by Lemma 2.1 (f ≢ 0 on [0, ∞) by (I1), and f is continuous).
179: 2. The Hermite–Genocchi components g_u(t) = (t − τ)² e^{−μ_u(t−τ)} of Lemma 3.4(5) are continuous and nonnegative,
180:    and vanish only at t = τ, so 0 < ∫ g_u. Here μ_u ∈ [μ_min, μ_max] := [min λ_i, max λ_i] ⊂ (0, ∞), so
181:    (1 + t²) g_u(t) ≤ (1 + t²)(t − τ)² e^{μ_max|τ|} e^{−μ_min t} uniformly in u, and ∫_Σ ∫_0^∞ (1 + t²) g_u dt du < ∞.
182: 
183: Hence no application uses the pointwise reading of "nonzero".
184: 
185: **Lemma 2.4 (extreme rays).** Let E be admissible with K_E ≠ {0}.
186: 1. min_{K_E∖0} SCV exists.
187: 2. Every f ∈ K_E∖0 has finitely many f_i on extreme rays of K_E with SCV(f) ≥ min_i SCV(f_i).
188: 3. The minimum in (1) is attained on an extreme ray.
189: 
190: Hence inf_{K_E∖0} SCV = inf over the extreme rays of K_E.
191: 
192: *Proof.* SCV is scale-invariant and continuous on K_E∖0: the m_k are linear on V_E, and m0, m1 > 0 there. So it
193: attains its minimum on the compact B_E (2.1, I6). Extreme points of B_E lie on extreme rays: if e = x + y with
194: x, y ∈ K_E∖0, then e = ℓ(x)·x/ℓ(x) + ℓ(y)·y/ℓ(y) with ℓ(x) + ℓ(y) = 1, so x/ℓ(x) = y/ℓ(y) = e. By (I5),
195: f/ℓ(f) = Σ c_i e_i with e_i extreme in B_E and c_i > 0; apply 2.3. Applying (2) to a minimizer gives (3). ∎
196: 
197: **Lemma 2.5 (bookkeeping; correction of the outline).** If E ⊆ E' (both admissible), then V_E ⊆ V_{E'} and
198: K_E = K_{E'} ∩ V_E ⊆ K_{E'}. Hence inf_{K_{E'}∖0} SCV ≤ inf_{K_E∖0} SCV. Every real admissible E with |E| ≤ 2 lies in
199: a real admissible E' with |E'| = 3: add real negative numbers outside E, or repeat one.
200: **K_E need not be a face of K_{E'}.** Counterexample: E = {−1} and E' = {−1, −2, −3}. Then x := e^{−t} − e^{−3t} and
201: y := e^{−t} + e^{−3t} lie in K_{E'} (e^{−t} ≥ e^{−3t} on [0, ∞)), and x + y = 2e^{−t} ∈ K_E, but x, y ∉ V_E. Only the
202: inclusion is used below. K_E *is* a face when E' ∖ E is the asymptotically dominant basis element (Lemma 3.2).
203: 
204: **Corollary 2.6 (assembly).** Define three infima of SCV:
205: * I_R over K_E∖0 with E real admissible;
206: * I_1 over the D1 cones K_{{−1, −1±iω}}, ω > 0;
207: * I_2 over the D2 cones K_{{−1, −(1+δ)±iω}}, δ, ω > 0.
208: 
209: Then inf{SCV(X) : X ∈ ME(≤3) continuous} = min(I_R, I_1, I_2), and each of the three infima is an infimum over the
210: extreme rays of the respective cones.
211: 
212: *Proof.* A density f lies in B_{E(f)}. E(f) is of type R or D, and SCV is invariant under the time change (normal
213: forms). Type R with |E(f)| < 3 is padded (2.5). Conversely, every nonzero cone element, normalized, is an ME(≤3)
214: density (L1(b)). A complex E whose pair is not dominated by the real exponent has K_E = K_{{λ0}} by Lemma 1.2, which is
215: contained in the real case. Finally apply 2.4. ∎
216: 
217: ---
218: 
219: ## 3. L3 — real exponents (cases A, B, C)
220: 
221: **Setting.** E is a real admissible multiset with |E| = 3, written with positive *rates*. The basis φ1, φ2, φ3 is
222: ordered by decreasing dominance at +∞:
223: * (A) E = {−λ1, −λ2, −λ3}, 0 < λ1 < λ2 < λ3: φ1 = e^{−λ1t}, φ2 = e^{−λ2t}, φ3 = e^{−λ3t}.
224: * (B1) E = {−λ, −λ, −μ}, 0 < λ < μ: φ1 = t e^{−λt}, φ2 = e^{−λt}, φ3 = e^{−μt}.
225: * (B2) E = {−λ, −μ, −μ}, 0 < λ < μ: φ1 = e^{−λt}, φ2 = t e^{−μt}, φ3 = e^{−μt}.
226: * (C) E = {−λ, −λ, −λ}: φ1 = t² e^{−λt}, φ2 = t e^{−λt}, φ3 = e^{−λt}.
227: 
228: For f = a1φ1 + a2φ2 + a3φ3 put a1(f) := a1. Let λ_* be the smallest rate, m_* its multiplicity, and
229: ψ(t) := (1 + t)^{m_*−1} e^{−λ_* t} > 0. Two facts follow by inspection of each type: (F1) sup_{t≥0} |g|/ψ < ∞ for
230: every g ∈ V_E; (F2) f(t)/ψ(t) → a1(f) as t → ∞.
231: 
232: **Lemma 3.1 (zero count; Chebyshev property).** Let E be any real multiset of size N ≥ 1. Every nonzero g ∈ V_E has at
233: most N − 1 real zeros counted with multiplicity.
234: 
235: *Proof.* Induction on N; N = 1 is trivial. Write g = Σ_{i=1}^r p_i(t) e^{κ_i t} with κ_i the distinct elements of E,
236: multiplicities m_i and deg p_i ≤ m_i − 1 (some p_i may be 0). If at most one p_i ≠ 0, then g = p_i e^{κ_i t} has
237: ≤ m_i − 1 ≤ N − 1 zeros. Otherwise let p_j ≠ 0 for some j ≥ 2. Put h := e^{−κ1 t} g; it has the same zeros with the
238: same multiplicities. Then h^{(m1)} = Σ_{i≥2} p̃_i e^{(κ_i−κ1)t} with deg p̃_i = deg p_i, because κ_i − κ1 ≠ 0 and
239: (D + c) preserves degree for c ≠ 0. So h^{(m1)} ≠ 0 lies in a real e.p. space of size N − m1 and, by induction, has
240: ≤ N − m1 − 1 zeros. Rolle with multiplicities (I3) says: if h has zeros x1 < … < xs of multiplicities k_j with
241: Σ k_j = Z, then h' has a zero of multiplicity ≥ k_j − 1 at each x_j and a zero in each (x_j, x_{j+1}), so ≥ Z − 1
242: zeros. If h had ≥ N zeros (choose finitely many), h^{(m1)} would have ≥ N − m1 zeros, a contradiction. ∎
243: 
244: **Lemma 3.2 (dominant coefficient; face).** a1(f) ≥ 0 for f ∈ K_E. K' := K_E ∩ {a1 = 0} is an exposed face of K_E.
245: It is the nonnegative cone of V' := span(φ2, φ3) = V_{E''} for a real multiset E'' of size 2:
246: * (A) {e^{−λ2t}, e^{−λ3t}};
247: * (B1) {e^{−λt}, e^{−μt}};
248: * (B2) {t e^{−μt}, e^{−μt}};
249: * (C) {t e^{−λt}, e^{−λt}}.
250: 
251: The extreme rays of K_E inside K' are exactly the extreme rays of K'.
252: 
253: *Proof.* By (F2), a1(f) = lim f/ψ ≥ 0. If x + y ∈ K' with x, y ∈ K_E, then a1(x) + a1(y) = 0 with both ≥ 0, so
254: x, y ∈ K'. An extreme ray of K' is therefore extreme in K_E, and conversely. ∎
255: 
256: **Lemma 3.3 (face rays).**
257: 1. For 0 < κ1 < κ2, a e^{−κ1t} + b e^{−κ2t} ≥ 0 on [0, ∞) iff a ≥ 0 and a + b ≥ 0. The extreme rays are e^{−κ2t} and
258:    e^{−κ1t} − e^{−κ2t}.
259: 2. For κ > 0, (a + bt) e^{−κt} ≥ 0 on [0, ∞) iff a, b ≥ 0. The extreme rays are e^{−κt} and t e^{−κt}.
260: 3. SCV(e^{−κt}) = 1, SCV(t e^{−κt}) = 1/2, and SCV(e^{−κ1t} − e^{−κ2t}) = (κ1² + κ2²)/(κ1 + κ2)² ∈ [1/2, 1). So every
261:    face ray has SCV ≥ 1/2.
262: 
263: *Proof.* (1) e^{κ1t} f = a + b x, where x = e^{−(κ2−κ1)t} ranges over (0, 1]. An affine function is ≥ 0 on (0, 1] iff
264: it is ≥ 0 as x → 0+ and at x = 1. The planar cone {a ≥ 0, a + b ≥ 0} has extreme rays (0, 1) and (1, −1).
265: (2) t = 0 gives a ≥ 0 and t → ∞ gives b ≥ 0; the converse is clear.
266: (3) m_k(e^{−κt}) = k!/κ^{k+1}, m_k(t e^{−κt}) = (k+1)!/κ^{k+2} and m_k(e^{−κ1t} − e^{−κ2t}) = k!(κ1^{−k−1} − κ2^{−k−1}).
267: For the last, m0m2/m1² = 2(κ1² + κ1κ2 + κ2²)/(κ1 + κ2)². Finally 2(κ1² + κ2²) ≥ (κ1 + κ2)². ∎
268: 
269: **Lemma 3.4 (double-zero rays; Hermite–Genocchi).** Let (λ1, λ2, λ3) list the rates of E with multiplicity. For τ ∈ ℝ
270: define F_τ(t) := (t − τ)² ∫_Σ e^{−(t−τ)(u1λ1 + u2λ2 + u3λ3)} du2 du3. Then:
271: 1. F_τ = ϕ_{t−τ}[λ1, λ2, λ3] with ϕ_s(x) := e^{−sx}, so F_τ ∈ V_E. For type A,
272:    F_τ(t) = Σ_i e^{−λ_i(t−τ)} / Π_{j≠i}(λ_i − λ_j).
273: 2. F_τ > 0 on ℝ∖{τ}, F_τ(τ) = F_τ'(τ) = 0 and F_τ''(τ) = 1.
274: 3. Every g ∈ V_E with g(τ) = g'(τ) = 0 is a real multiple of F_τ.
275: 4. a1(F_τ) > 0.
276: 5. SCV(F_τ) ≥ Δ3 := inf_{σ∈ℝ} SCV((t − σ)² e^{−t}) for every τ ∈ ℝ.
277: 
278: *Proof.*
279: 1. (I4) with ϕ_s'' = s² e^{−sx}. The divided difference is a node-dependent combination of
280:    ϕ_s^{(j)}(λ_i) = (−s)^j e^{−sλ_i} with j < mult λ_i. With s = t − τ each such term equals
281:    e^{λ_iτ} (τ − t)^j e^{−λ_i t} ∈ V_E.
282: 2. F_τ = (t − τ)² k(t), where k := ∫_Σ e^{−(t−τ)⟨u,λ⟩} > 0 is C^∞ and k(τ) = area Σ = 1/2.
283: 3. g ↦ (g(τ), g'(τ)) has kernel W ∋ F_τ with dim W ≥ 1. If dim W ≥ 2, then for any t1 ≠ τ some 0 ≠ g ∈ W has
284:    g(t1) = 0. That is three zeros counted with multiplicity, contradicting 3.1 (N = 3).
285: 4. If a1(F_τ) = 0, then F_τ would be a nonzero element of the size-2 space V' with a double zero, contradicting 3.1
286:    (N = 2). So a1(F_τ) ≥ 0 by (F2), and a1(F_τ) ≠ 0.
287: 5. F_τ = ∫_Σ g_u du with g_u(t) := (t − τ)² e^{−μ_u(t−τ)} and μ_u := ⟨u, λ⟩ ∈ [min λ_i, max λ_i] ⊂ (0, ∞). By 1.4
288:    (t ↦ t/μ_u), SCV(g_u) = SCV((t − μ_uτ)² e^{−t}) ≥ Δ3. The integrability in 2.3 holds because the integrand is
289:    continuous in u on the compact Σ. ∎
290: 
291: `ctl-a-v1` (a3) checks exactly (sympy) that s²∫_Σ e^{−s⟨u,λ⟩} equals the (confluent) divided difference for the node
292: triples (1,2,3), (1,1,2), (1,2,2) and (1,1,1), with quotient by s² → 1/2 at s = 0.
293: 
294: **Lemma 3.5 (classification of extreme rays).** Every extreme ray of K_E (E real, |E| = 3) is one of:
295: * (a) the ray of F_τ for some τ ≥ 0;
296: * (b) one of the two face rays of 3.3. These are e^{−λ3t} and e^{−λ2t} − e^{−λ3t} (A); e^{−μt} and e^{−λt} − e^{−μt}
297:   (B1); e^{−μt} and t e^{−μt} (B2); e^{−λt} and t e^{−λt} (C).
298: 
299: Conversely, every F_τ with τ ≥ 0 spans an extreme ray.
300: 
301: *Proof.* Let f ∈ K_E∖0 span an extreme ray. If a1(f) = 0, use 3.2 and 3.3. Let a1(f) > 0. By 3.1, f has ≤ 2 real
302: zeros counted with multiplicity. Zeros in (0, ∞) have even multiplicity (≥ 2), since f ≥ 0 nearby and f is analytic.
303: So on [0, ∞) exactly one of the following holds:
304: * (P0) no zero;
305: * (P1) only t = 0, with multiplicity 1;
306: * (P2) only t = 0, with multiplicity 2;
307: * (P3) only one τ > 0, with multiplicity 2.
308: 
309: * (P0) f/ψ is continuous and positive on [0, ∞), and → a1 > 0 (F2), so f ≥ cψ with c > 0. Every g ∈ V_E has
310:   |g| ≤ Mψ (F1). Choose g ∉ span f (dim 3). Then f ± (c/M)g ∈ K_E, so f = ½(f + εg) + ½(f − εg) is not extreme.
311: * (P1) f(0) = 0 < f'(0). Put ψ0 := (t/(1 + t))ψ. f/ψ0 → f'(0) > 0 at 0+ and → a1 > 0 at ∞, and it is positive in
312:   between, so f ≥ cψ0 with c > 0. Choose g ∉ span f with g(0) = 0 (a 2-dimensional subspace). Then |g| ≤ Mψ0: near 0,
313:   |g(t)| ≤ t·sup|g'|; near ∞ use (F1). As in (P0), f is not extreme.
314: * (P2), (P3) f(τ) = f'(τ) = 0 with τ ≥ 0, so f = c F_τ with c > 0 (3.4(3), and F_τ > 0 off τ).
315: 
316: Converse: let F_τ = x + y with x, y ∈ K_E. Then x(τ) = y(τ) = 0. If τ > 0, τ is an interior minimum of x, y ≥ 0, so
317: x'(τ) = y'(τ) = 0. If τ = 0, then x'(0), y'(0) ≥ 0 and x'(0) + y'(0) = 0, so both vanish. By 3.4(3) x and y are
318: nonnegative multiples of F_τ. ∎
319: 
320: **Theorem L3.** Every continuous X ∈ ME(≤3) whose density has only real contributing exponents has SCV(X) ≥ Δ3. The
321: bound is attained: for E = {−1, −1, −1}, F_{x1} = ½ (t − x1)² e^{−(t − x1)} has SCV = Δ3, where x1 ≈ 0.41577 > 0 is
322: the smallest zero of the Laguerre polynomial L3 (L4). Hence I_R = Δ3.
323: 
324: *Proof.* Pad to |E| = 3 (2.5). Then 2.4, 3.5, 3.4(5) and 3.3(3) give SCV ≥ min(Δ3, 1/2) = Δ3, because
325: Δ3 ≤ SCV(t² e^{−t}) = 1/3 < 1/2. For type C, F_τ = ½ (t − τ)² e^{−(t−τ)}, so SCV(F_τ) = s(τ), and s(x1) = Δ3 by L4. ∎
326: 
327: ---
328: 
329: ## 4. L4 — the constant Δ3
330: 
331: Let s(τ) := SCV((t − τ)² e^{−t}), the density taken on [0, ∞).
332: 
333: **Theorem L4.**
334: 1. 1 + s(τ) = P(τ)/Q(τ)², with P := (τ² − 2τ + 2)(2τ² − 12τ + 24) and Q := τ² − 4τ + 6 = (τ − 2)² + 2 > 0.
335: 2. 4(P − (5/4)Q²) = 3(τ² − 4τ + 2)² + 8τ² > 0 for all real τ. Hence s(τ) > 1/4 on ℝ, and Δ3 > 1/4 (the infimum is
336:    attained by (3)).
337: 3. s'(τ) = 8q(τ)/Q³, with q(τ) := τ³ − 9τ² + 18τ − 6 = −6 L3(τ) (so s' = −48 L3/Q³), where L3(x) = 1 − 3x + 3x²/2 − x³/6
338:    is the Laguerre polynomial; and s → 1 at ±∞.
339:    * q has exactly one root in each of (0, 1/2), (2, 3) and (6, 7); call them x1 < x2 < x3 (the zeros of L3).
340:    * Δ3 = min_ℝ s = s(x1) ∈ (1/4, 1/3).
341:    * Δ3 = (x1(9 − x1)² − 24)/24 = (x3 − x2)²/(4 x2 x3).
342: 
343:    The last form is the "relative gap of the Laguerre L3 zeros" of Battagliola–Peralta (arXiv:2608.29143); this agrees
344:    exactly, and is not used as a premise.
345: 4. Δ3 is a root of h(y) := 32y³ − 144y² + 135y − 27, and h is irreducible over ℚ.
346:    * COMPUTATIONAL (conditional; run `ctl-b-v1`; supplementary, used by no proof): lo = 0.2765825307809456 < Δ3 <
347:      hi = 0.2765825307809457 (the lower bracket relies on a SymPy Sturm count); the Arb enclosure
348:      Δ3 = 0.27658253078094566646323160988256346700325763105135079308086826963083 ± 2.41·10⁻⁷¹;
349:      x1 = 0.41577455678347908331153387312827… and 1/Δ3 = 3.61555734260022193537328283351….
350:    * EXACT (Lemma 4.2 and its run): Δ3 > 553/2000, and the 18-digit bracket Δ3 = 0.276582530780945666….
351: 
352: *Proof.*
353: 1. m_k = (k+2)! − 2τ(k+1)! + τ² k!, so m0 = τ² − 2τ + 2, m1 = Q and m2 = 2τ² − 12τ + 24.
354: 2. Expansion: both sides equal 3τ⁴ − 24τ³ + 68τ² − 48τ + 12. The sum of squares vanishes only if τ = 0 and
355:    τ² − 4τ + 2 = 0 simultaneously, which is impossible.
356: 3. d/dτ(P/Q²) = (P'Q − 2PQ')/Q³. Expansion gives P'Q − 2PQ' = (8τ⁵ − 80τ⁴ + 344τ³ − 776τ² + 912τ − 432) −
357:    (8τ⁵ − 80τ⁴ + 336τ³ − 704τ² + 768τ − 384) = 8q.
358:    * Root isolation: q(0) = −6, q(1/2) = 7/8, q(2) = 2, q(3) = −6, q(6) = −6 and q(7) = 22. So q has a root in each of
359:      (0, 1/2), (2, 3) and (6, 7). A cubic has no more roots, so these three roots are all simple, q < 0 on
360:      (−∞, x1) ∪ (x2, x3), and q > 0 on (x1, x2) ∪ (x3, ∞).
361:    * Monotonicity: s decreases on (−∞, x1], increases on [x1, x2], decreases on [x2, x3] and increases on [x3, ∞).
362:      Since deg P = deg Q² = 4 with leading coefficients 2 and 1, s → 1 at ±∞. So min_ℝ s exists and equals
363:      min(s(x1), s(x3)).
364:    * Since 0 < x1 and s is strictly decreasing on (−∞, x1], s(x1) < s(0) = 48/36 − 1 = 1/3.
365:    * For τ ≥ 6, s(τ) − 1/2 = (2P − 3Q²)/(2Q²), with 2P − 3Q² = τ⁴ − 8τ³ + 20τ² − 12 = τ²((τ − 4)² + 4) − 12 ≥
366:      36·4 − 12 > 0. So s(x3) > 1/2.
367:    * Thus Δ3 = s(x1) < 1/3, and Δ3 > 1/4 by (2): s > 1/4 pointwise and the minimum is attained.
368:    * The closed forms: the numerator of s − (x(9 − x)² − 24)/24 has remainder exactly 0 modulo q. Vieta
369:      (x2 + x3 = 9 − x1, x2x3 = 6/x1) turns (x3 − x2)²/(4x2x3) into ((9 − x1)² − 24/x1)/(24/x1) =
370:      (x1(9 − x1)² − 24)/24.
371: 4. Since s = (P − Q²)/Q², the polynomial Q⁶·h(s) = 32(P − Q²)³ − 144(P − Q²)²Q² + 135(P − Q²)Q⁴ − 27Q⁶ has
372:    remainder exactly 0 modulo q. So h(s(x)) = 0 at every root x of q (Q(x) > 0); in particular h(Δ3) = 0.
373:    Equivalently, h/32 is the resultant in x of q(x) and 24y − x(9 − x)² + 24.
374:    Irreducibility: a rational root of h has denominator dividing 32, hence prime to 5, so it would give a root of
375:    h ≡ 2y³ + y² + 3 (mod 5). That polynomial has the values 3, 1, 3, 1, 2 at y = 0, …, 4, so it has no root. ∎
376: 
377: **Lemma 4.2 (exact lower bound; replaces the v1 Sturm certificate).** Δ3 > 553/2000 = 0.2765.
378: 
379: *Proof.* h'(y) = 96y² − 288y + 135 = 96(y − 3/2)² − 81. For y ∈ [1/4, 1/3] we have 3/2 − y ≥ 7/6, so
380: h'(y) ≥ 96·(7/6)² − 81 = 149/3 > 0; this is h'(1/3), the minimum of h' on the interval. Hence h is strictly increasing
381: on [1/4, 1/3], and Δ3 ∈ (1/4, 1/3) is its unique root there (L4(3)–(4); consistently, h(1/4) = −7/4 < 0 <
382: 86/27 = h(1/3)). Since 553/2000 ∈ [1/4, 1/3] and h(553/2000) = −1293623/250000000 < 0 = h(Δ3), we get 553/2000 < Δ3. ∎
383: 
384: *EXACT certificate (run `exact-certs-v1`).*
385: * Checks C2a–C2g and the SymPy cross-checks X_* verify:
386:   * the moment polynomials and Q = (τ − 2)² + 2;
387:   * the SOS identity (no common zero);
388:   * P'Q − 2PQ' = 8q and the six q values;
389:   * the leading coefficients, s(0) = 1/3, and the identity for 2P − 3Q²;
390:   * Q⁶h(s) ≡ 0 and s ≡ (x(9 − x)² − 24)/24 modulo q;
391:   * the h' identity and the bound 149/3;
392:   * h(1/4), h(1/3) and h(553/2000).
393: * 60 exact bisection steps on [1/4, 1/3] give the EXACT bracket
394:   Δ3 ∈ (478316921303906167/1729382256910270464, 1275511790143749779/4611686018427387904), i.e.
395:   Δ3 = 0.276582530780945666… (18 digits).
396: * Wrong controls that fail as required:
397:   * h(2766/10000) = 4277637/3906250000 > 0, so it does not certify Δ3 > 0.2766 (it certifies Δ3 < 0.2766);
398:   * the point 1/5 lies outside the monotonicity window and is refused.
399: * h(552/2000) = −142803/3906250 < 0 certifies only the weaker, true bound Δ3 > 0.276, so it is not a failing control.
400: 
401: *Supplementary computation (COMPUTATIONAL, conditional on SymPy/Arb semantics; used by no proof):* run `ctl-b-v1`
402: (exact sympy + arb, 512 bits), 24/24 checks. Four mutations were each rejected for the intended reason:
403: * claim s > 0.2766 everywhere: 2 real roots, with the exact witness s(21/50) = 11650027/42120027 < 0.2766;
404: * SOS coefficient 8 → 7: residual τ²;
405: * upper bound lo − 10⁻¹⁶: positive at τ0, with a Sturm proof that s exceeds it everywhere;
406: * wrong root box x2: s(x2) lies outside the bracket.

409: 
410: ## 5. L5 — common damping D1 and attainment of v_cos
411: 
412: **Setting.** E = {−1, −1 ± iω} with ω > 0 (after the time change). V = span{e^{−t}, e^{−t} cos ωt, e^{−t} sin ωt}.
413: Put f_{ω,φ}(t) := e^{−t}(1 − cos(ωt − φ)).
414: 
415: **Lemma 5.1 (cone, extreme rays).** K = {e^{−t}(a + b cos ωt + c sin ωt) : a ≥ √(b² + c²)}. Its extreme rays are
416: exactly the rays of f_{ω,φ}, φ ∈ ℝ mod 2π, with distinct φ giving distinct rays. Hence I_1 = inf_{ω>0, φ} SCV(f_{ω,φ}).
417: 
418: *Proof.* Write R := √(b² + c²) and b cos ωt + c sin ωt = R cos(ωt − χ). If a ≥ R, then f ≥ (a − R)e^{−t} ≥ 0.
419: Conversely, evaluating at t_k := (χ + π + 2πk)/ω ≥ 0 gives a − R ≥ 0. For extreme rays of {a ≥ |v|}, v ∈ ℝ²: let
420: (|v|, v) = (a1, v1) + (a2, v2) with a_i ≥ |v_i| and v ≠ 0. Then |v| = a1 + a2 ≥ |v1| + |v2| ≥ |v|, so a_i = |v_i|
421: and v1, v2 are nonnegative multiples of v. If a > |v|, then
422: (a, v) = (a − |v|)(1, 0) + (|v|, v) (or, when v = 0, (a/2)(1, e) + (a/2)(1, −e)) is not extreme. Finally
423: R e^{−t}(1 + cos(ωt − χ)) = R f_{ω, χ+π}. ∎
424: 
425: **Lemma 5.2 (closed form).** m_k(f_{ω,φ}) = k!(1 − Re[e^{−iφ}(1 − iω)^{−(k+1)}]) = k!(1 − ρ^{k+1} cos((k+1)β − φ)),
426: with ρ := (1 + ω²)^{−1/2} ∈ (0, 1) and β := arctan ω ∈ (0, π/2). In particular m0, m1, m2 > 0.
427: 
428: *Proof.* cos(ωt − φ) = Re e^{i(ωt−φ)}, and ∫_0^∞ t^k e^{−(1−iω)t} dt = k!(1 − iω)^{−(k+1)}; also 1/(1 − iω) = ρe^{iβ}. ∎
429: 
430: **Lemma 5.3 (algebraic form).** Put ψ := φ − 2β, C := cos ψ, S := sin ψ, u := 1 − C ∈ [0, 2], X := ω² + u,
431: a := ω²(1 + ω²) and Y := ωS, so that Y² = ω²u(2 − u). Then m0 = (X + Y)/(1 + ω²), m1 = X/(1 + ω²),
432: m2 = 2(X + a − Y)/(1 + ω²)², and
433:   1 + SCV(f_{ω,φ}) = 2(X + Y)(X + a − Y)/((1 + ω²)X²) = 2(X² + aX + aY − Y²)/((1 + ω²)X²).
434: 
435: *Proof.* e^{−iφ}(1 − iω)^{−(k+1)} = ρ^{k+1} e^{−iψ} e^{i(k−1)β}, with ρe^{iβ} = q(1 + iω), ρe^{−iβ} = q(1 − iω) and
436: ρ² = q := 1/(1 + ω²). The real parts are q(C − ωS), qC and q²(C + ωS) for k = 0, 1, 2. Then 1 − q(C − ωS) =
437: (X + Y)q, 1 − qC = Xq and 1 − q²(C + ωS) = ((1 + ω²)² − 1 + u − Y)q² = (X + a − Y)q². ∎
438: (`ctl-c-v2` c5 checks the identity with arb at 1056 rational sample points; the maximum ball radius of the difference
439: is 5.3·10⁻⁹², and every ball contains 0.)
440: 
441: **Lemma 5.4 (uniform bound).** For all ω > 0 and φ ∈ ℝ: 1 + SCV(f_{ω,φ}) ≥ (1 + Δ3)/(1 + ω²), i.e.
442: SCV ≥ (Δ3 − ω²)/(1 + ω²). In particular, for ω ≤ 1/4, SCV ≥ (16Δ3 − 1)/17 > (16·553/2000 − 1)/17 = 428/2125 =
443: 0.2014117… > 0.2014 (by Lemma 4.2; EXACT).
444: 
445: *Proof.*
446: 1. Put Y_m := ω√(u(2 − u)) ≥ 0. Then Y² = Y_m² and Y ≥ −Y_m, so with a > 0,
447:    X² + aX + aY − Y² ≥ X² + aX − aY_m − Y_m².
448: 2. Write u = ω²v with v ∈ [0, 2/ω²]. Then X = ω²(1 + v), a = ω²(1 + ω²), Y_m = ω²√(v(2 − ω²v)), and the right side of
449:    step 1 equals ω⁴ N(ω, v), where
450:    N(ω, v) := (1 + v)² + (1 + ω²)(1 + v − √(v(2 − ω²v))) − v(2 − ω²v).
451: 3. 0 ≤ v(2 − ω²v) ≤ 2v and √(2v) ≤ 1 + v, so 1 + v − √(v(2 − ω²v)) ≥ 1 + v − √(2v) ≥ 0. Hence
452:    N(ω, v) ≥ N0(v) := (1 + v)² + 1 + v − √(2v) − 2v.
453: 4. Let σ := √(2v). Then 2N0/(1 + v)² = 2(σ⁴ + 2σ² − 4σ + 8)/(σ² + 2)² = P(2 − σ)/Q(2 − σ)² = 1 + s(2 − σ) ≥ 1 + Δ3.
454:    This uses P(2 − σ) = (σ² − 2σ + 2)·2(σ² + 2σ + 4) = 2(σ⁴ + 2σ² − 4σ + 8) and Q(2 − σ) = σ² + 2.
455: 5. So 1 + SCV ≥ 2ω⁴N/((1 + ω²)ω⁴(1 + v)²) ≥ (1 + Δ3)/(1 + ω²). The bound (Δ3 − ω²)/(1 + ω²) decreases in ω. ∎
456: 
457: The bound is sharp as ω → 0 (EXPLORATORY `ctl-c-v2` c5: at ω = 10⁻², min_φ SCV ≈ 0.276563 against the bound
458: 0.276455). It also holds with certified margin at all 1056 arb sample points (minimum gap 0.0446 at ω = 1/5).
459: 
460: **Lemma 5.5 (large ω).** For all ω > 0 and φ: 1 + SCV ≥ 2(1 − ρ)(1 − ρ³)/(1 + ρ²)², and the right side decreases in
461: ρ ∈ [0, 1). For ω ≥ 4: ρ ≤ 17^{−1/2} < ρ̄ := 0.2426, since ρ̄² = 0.05885476 > 1/17. Hence
462: SCV ≥ 2(1 − ρ̄)(1 − ρ̄³)/(1 + ρ̄²)² − 1 = 232498770764161/700733376734161 = 0.33179… > 0.33 (EXACT; ρ̄² = 1471369/25000000;
463: re-checked in `exact-certs-v1`, C1).
464: 
465: *Proof.* From 5.2: m0 ≥ 1 − ρ > 0, m1 ≤ 1 + ρ² and m2 ≥ 2(1 − ρ³) > 0. The numerator factors are positive and
466: decreasing in ρ, and the denominator is increasing. ∎
467: 
468: **Theorem 5.6 (attainment; exact upper separation).** Let φ_e ∈ (0, π/2) be the angle with cos φ_e = 35/37 and
469: sin φ_e = 12/37; it exists since 35² + 12² = 37². Then (EXACT) f_{1,φ_e} has moments
470:   (m0, m1, m2) = (27/74, 62/74, 171/74),  SCV(f_{1,φ_e}) = 773/3844 = 0.20109261…,
471: so **v_cos ≤ 773/3844 < 1007/5000 = 0.2014 < 11/50 = T**, and 773/3844 < 553/2000 < Δ3. Moreover v_cos is attained,
472: every minimizer has 1/4 < ω* < 4, and every (ω, φ) with ω ≤ 1/4 or ω ≥ 4 has SCV > 0.2014.
473: 
474: *Proof.*
475: * Moments, by Lemma 5.2 with ω = 1: (1 − i)^{−1} = (1 + i)/2, (1 − i)^{−2} = i/2, (1 − i)^{−3} = (−1 + i)/4, and
476:   e^{−iφ_e} = (35 − 12i)/37. The real parts of the three products are 47/74, 12/74 and −23/148. So m0 = 1 − 47/74 =
477:   27/74, m1 = 1 − 12/74 = 62/74 and m2 = 2(1 + 23/148) = 171/74, and SCV = 27·171/62² − 1 = 4617/3844 − 1 = 773/3844.
478: * Comparisons: 773·5000 = 3865000 < 3870908 = 1007·3844, and 1007/5000 < 11/50.
479: * Attainment: SCV is continuous on (0, ∞) × ℝ (5.2, m1 > 0) and 2π-periodic in φ. So its minimum m* over the
480:   compact set [1/4, 4] × [0, 2π] is attained, and m* ≤ SCV(1, φ_e) = 773/3844.
481: * Tails: by Lemma 5.4 with Lemma 4.2, every point with ω ≤ 1/4 has SCV ≥ 428/2125 > 773/3844 (773·2125 = 1642625 <
482:   1645232 = 428·3844). By Lemma 5.5, every point with ω ≥ 4 has SCV > 0.33 > 773/3844.
483: * Hence m* = v_cos, it is attained, and every minimizer has 1/4 < ω* < 4. ∎
484: 
485: *EXACT certificate (run `exact-certs-v1`, checks C1_*).*
486: * Verified: the Pythagorean identity; the moments from Lemma 5.2; the same moments independently from Lemma 5.3's
487:   algebraic form and from exact SymPy integration of ∫_0^∞ t^k e^{−t}(1 − (35/37) cos t − (12/37) sin t) dt;
488:   SCV = 773/3844; and every comparison above.
489: * Wrong controls that fail as required: swapped (cos, sin) = (12/37, 35/37) gives SCV = 206/169; the phase-sign
490:   mutation e^{+iφ} gives 2549/7396; and (35/37, 13/37) violates c² + s² = 1.
491: 
492: Hence the definition "v_cos := min" in the contract is well posed.
493: 
494: *Supplementary (COMPUTATIONAL, conditional on python-flint Arb/Acb semantics; used by no proof).* At the rational
495: point ω_U = 517966477543/500000000000, φ_U = 674076484769/2000000000000, the Arb ball (`ctl-c-v2` c3, prec 320) is
496: SCV ∈ 0.200901563500556958801380682455159321656863180 ± 2.1·10⁻⁴⁶. So v_cos < U := 0.2009015635005569588013806824561593216569.
497: 
498: **Numerical value (EXPLORATORY, `ctl-c-v2` c1; Newton at 80 digits from the parent point, Hessian positive definite
499: with eigenvalues 0.26860 and 1.29634).** The stationary point is:
500: * ω* = 1.0359329550858567909065869278226347339723…
501: * φ* = 0.3370382423844937614264527712363442589648…
502: * v_cos = 0.200901563500556958801380678959513327689390751533559694715965
503: * 1/v_cos = 4.977562058630906090494547025747273…
504: 
505: Sign check: at the stationary point ψ* = −1.26905…, S = sin ψ* < 0, which is the minimizing sign of Lemma 5.4's
506: proof. Global minimality of this stationary point is not certified here. What is proved is attainment and
507: v_cos ≤ 773/3844 (EXACT); v_cos < U is conditional. A float grid (1501 × 2400 points on [1/4, 4] × [0, 2π)) shows no
508: other basin within 10⁻⁴ of the minimum.
509: 
510: **Contract band (EXPLORATORY; relabelled in v2).**
511: * What it is: the check `c1_v_cos_in_contract_band_[0.20090144,0.20090185]` of `ctl-c-v2` (code
512:   `ctl_c_vcos_v2.py` LF102–103; output `ctl_c.json` LF122–125) compares the *Newton stationary value* with the band.
513: * Why it is not a certificate: a stationary value is not known to be the global minimum, so the check cannot
514:   establish the lower band endpoint. The code docstring (LF10–15) wrongly lists the band check under CERTIFIED.
515: * Actual stop behaviour: if the value were outside the band, LF126–127 only print "STOP: …" and the script continues.
516:   The failure would reach the exit code only through `all_pass` (exit 1).
517: * What is certified about the band:
518:   * the upper endpoint, conditionally, via U < 0.20090185 (Arb, supplementary);
519:   * the lower endpoint only via the optional Theorem 5.8 (COMPUTATIONAL, conditional);
520:   * the exact upper bound 773/3844 = 0.20109… lies above the band, so it certifies neither endpoint.
521: * The theorem needs neither endpoint.
522: 
523: **Corollary 5.7.** Every continuous X ∈ ME(≤3) of type D1 has SCV(X) ≥ v_cos, with equality for f_{ω*,φ*}, so
524: I_1 = v_cos. Every type-R law has SCV ≥ Δ3 > 553/2000 > 1007/5000 > 773/3844 ≥ v_cos (all EXACT: Lemma 4.2 and
525: Theorem 5.6).
526: 
527: **Convention control (`ctl-d-v1`, arb, 12/12 checks).** The literal reading of HSTZ eq. (4) with the Table 2 numbers,
528: e^{−t} cos²(1.03593t − 0.337037), has SCV = 1.5757069924266101935 ± 4·10⁻²⁰. That is inconsistent with Table 1
529: (cv = 0.20090), so the reading is rejected as a convention error. The reading e^{−t}(1 − cos(1.03593t − 0.337037))
530: gives SCV = 0.20090156350197712143 ± 4·10⁻²¹ (1/SCV = 4.97756205859572), equal to v_cos + 1.4·10⁻¹² because the table
531: values are rounded. The identity 1 − cos x = 2 cos²(x/2 − π/2) means the cos² form with half frequency ω/2 and phase
532: φ/2 + π/2 is the same family; arb gives the identical SCV ball and moments in ratio exactly 1/2. Independent mpmath
533: quadrature agrees to 25 digits.
534: 
535: **Theorem 5.8 (OPTIONAL; COMPUTATIONAL, conditional; used by no proof).** 0.20090156350055 ≤ v_cos. The method and
536: proof are in `vcos-lower-bound-v1.md` (unchanged).

539: * Record: `institute/runs/six-lane-2026-10-16/review/me3-parent-primal-reductions-v1/RESULT-v1.json`, sha256
540:   9a72e3e7133de30301785af96604b31838b9726d94055717291f3111744c65e3; re-hashed by this worker, 2026-10-18 06:10 PDT.
541: * It records run `d1b-lower-worker-L` as "CERTIFIED v_cos >= 0.20090156350055 (OPTIONAL)", with RESULT.json sha256
542:   aac0149d…395f, and run `d1a-lower-band` as "CERTIFIED v_cos >= 0.20090144 (OPTIONAL)".

545: **Decision after the v2 audit (review item 1): KEEP as optional and conditional.** The result is conditional on
546: python-flint 0.9.0 Arb/Acb outward rounding and comparison semantics, and on SymPy `simplify` returning 0 for the
547: gradient identities of `ctl-f-gradsym-v1`. The acceptance logic of the pinned program `code/ctl_f_d1bnb_v1.py`
548: (sha256 508b1488…41b7) was audited statically and against the library (run `audit-bnb-v2`, 27/27 checks):
549: * **Completion is an EMPTY unresolved stack, not a count.** The only success flag is
550:   `certified = bool(middle_certified and tails_ok)` (LF166), with
551:   `middle_certified = (not aborted) and (refuted is None) and not stack` (LF165).
552: * **No box leaves the stack uncertified.** Every `continue` in the loop is guarded by a certain ball comparison:
553:   `F_ball(W, U) > La` (LF135) or `(Fc − err) > La` (LF149, tried only when 0 < u_lo and u_hi < 2, LF146). Every other
554:   box is either bisected into both exact halves (LF156–163) or ends the loop with `aborted = True` (box cap LF129–131,
555:   depth cap LF152–155) or a refutation (LF143–145).
556: * **A cap, limit or indeterminate evaluation can never be reported as success.**
557:   * Aborted runs and refutations make `middle_certified` false.
558:   * python-flint comparisons are True only when certain. Undecided, NaN and infinite comparisons return False
559:     (audit C1). So an indeterminate enclosure is split, never certified: the full middle box gives F_ball = NaN
560:     (audit C4), and a gradient ball touching u = 0 gives NaN (audit C6).
561:   * Demonstrated by `ctl-f-mut-cap-v1`: box cap 1000, then aborted with 6 boxes left on the stack, outcome reject.
562:     The earlier mutations also reject: a refuted L, and a failed tail.
563: * **The tails are exact** (LF55–57). The tail input Δ3 > 2765/10000 is now also the EXACT Lemma 4.2.
564: * **Run evidence.** `ctl-f-main-v1` has aborted = false, refutation = null, remaining_stack = 0 and tails OK. Its
565:   processed-box count is 38439 = 2·19220 − 1, consistent with a complete binary tree with 19220 certified leaves.
566: * `audit-bnb-v1` (exit 1) is kept as a failed attempt. It wrongly expected a finite enclosure on the full middle box,
567:   an audit-expectation error; v2 checks the property that matters, namely that the box is not certified.

573: ## 6. L6 — the D2 cone (real pole strictly dominant)
574: 
575: **Setting.** E = {−1, −(1 + δ) ± iω} with δ > 0, ω > 0, and r := δ/ω. Elements of V are e^{−t}H with
576: H(t) = a + e^{−δt}(b cos ωt + c sin ωt). For τ ∈ ℝ put
577:   h_τ(t) := 1 − e^{−δ(t−τ)}(cos ω(t − τ) + r sin ω(t − τ)),  g_r(u) := e^{ru}(cos u − r sin u).
578: 
579: **Lemma 6.1.** a > 0 for every f = e^{−t}H ∈ K∖0.
580: *Proof.* H → a as t → ∞, so a ≥ 0. If a = 0, then e^{−δt}R cos(ωt − χ) ≥ 0 on [0, ∞), which forces R = 0 and f = 0. ∎
581: 
582: **Lemma 6.2 (double zero ⇒ unique ray).** h_τ ∈ span{1, e^{−δt} cos ωt, e^{−δt} sin ωt}, and
583: h_τ'(t) = ((ω² + δ²)/ω) e^{−δ(t−τ)} sin ω(t − τ), so h_τ(τ) = h_τ'(τ) = 0. The set {g ∈ V : g(τ) = g'(τ) = 0} is
584: the line spanned by e^{−t}h_τ.
585: *Proof.* Write g = e^{−t}G; then g(τ) = g'(τ) = 0 iff G(τ) = G'(τ) = 0.
586: G' = e^{−δt}[(−δb + ωc) cos ωt + (−ωb − δc) sin ωt]. The map (b, c) ↦ (−δb + ωc, −ωb − δc) has determinant
587: δ² + ω² > 0, so G'(τ) = 0 is one nontrivial linear condition on (b, c), and G(τ) = 0 then fixes a. The formula for
588: h_τ' and the span membership are direct computations (`ctl-e-v1` e0, exact sympy). ∎
589: 
590: **Lemma 6.3 (extreme rays).** The extreme rays of K are exactly the rays of e^{−t}h_τ for the *feasible* τ ≥ 0, i.e.
591: those with h_τ ≥ 0 on [0, ∞).
592: *Proof.* Let f = e^{−t}H ∈ K∖0, so a > 0. The following cases are exhaustive.
593: * (a) H > 0 on [0, ∞). Since H → a > 0, inf H > 0, while |G| ≤ M for every g = e^{−t}G ∈ V. So f ± εg ∈ K and f is
594:   interior, not extreme.
595: * (b) H(0) = 0, H'(0) > 0 and H > 0 on (0, ∞). Then H ≥ c·t/(1 + t) with c > 0. Choose g ∉ span f with G(0) = 0;
596:   then |G| ≤ M t/(1 + t). So f is not extreme. *This is the outline's "boundary τ = 0 needs h'(0) = 0".*
597: * (c) H(0) = H'(0) = 0. Then f is a positive multiple of e^{−t}h_0 (6.2).
598: * (d) H(τ) = 0 for some τ > 0. Then H'(τ) = 0 (interior minimum), so f is a positive multiple of e^{−t}h_τ.
599: 
600: H(0) = 0 forces H'(0) ≥ 0. In (c) and (d), extremality follows exactly as in 3.5: if f = x + y with x, y ∈ K, then
601: x and y vanish to second order at τ, and 6.2 applies. ∎
602: 
603: **Lemma 6.4 (feasibility).** h_τ(0) = 1 − g_r(ωτ). For τ ≥ 0: τ is feasible iff φ := ωτ ∈ [0, θ1(r)], where θ1(r)
604: is the unique solution in (π, 2π) of g_r(u) = 1. Equivalently, for τ < 2π/ω, feasibility ⟺ h_τ(0) ≥ 0.
605: *Proof.* First, h_τ(0) = 1 − e^{δτ}(cos ωτ − r sin ωτ) = 1 − g_r(φ), since δτ = rφ.
606: g_r'(u) = −(1 + r²) e^{ru} sin u. So g_r decreases strictly on [0, π] and increases strictly on [π, 2π], with
607: g_r(0) = 1, g_r(π) = −e^{rπ} and g_r(2π) = e^{2πr} > 1. Hence θ1 exists and is unique, g_r < 1 on (0, θ1) and g_r > 1
608: on (θ1, 2π].
609: 
610: Put s := t − τ. By 6.2, h_τ' has the sign of sin ωs.
611: * On s ≥ 0, the local minima of h_τ are at s = 2πk/ω, with values 1 − e^{−2πkδ/ω} ≥ 0. So h_τ ≥ 0 on [τ, ∞).
612: * If τ ≥ 2π/ω, then t = τ − 2π/ω ≥ 0 gives h_τ = 1 − e^{2πr} < 0: infeasible (and φ ≥ 2π > θ1).
613: * If τ < 2π/ω: on s ∈ (−π/ω, 0), h_τ decreases to h_τ(τ) = 0, so h_τ > 0 there. On s ∈ (−2π/ω, −π/ω], h_τ is
614:   nondecreasing. Hence φ ≤ π is feasible, and for φ ∈ (π, 2π) the minimum over [0, τ) is h_τ(0): feasible ⟺
615:   g_r(φ) ≤ 1 ⟺ φ ≤ θ1. ∎
616: 
617: **Lemma 6.5 (θ1).** θ1 : (0, ∞) → (π, 2π) is C¹ and strictly decreasing, with θ1(0+) = 2π and θ1(∞) = π.
618: *Proof.* At the root, ∂_u g_r = −(1 + r²)e^{ru} sin u > 0 and ∂_r g_r = u g_r − e^{ru} sin u = u − e^{ru} sin u > 0,
619: because g_r = 1 there and sin u < 0 on (π, 2π). By (I7), θ1' = −∂_r g/∂_u g < 0. Fix u ∈ (π, 2π).
620: * As r → 0+, g_r(u) → cos u < 1, so θ1(r) > u for small r. Hence θ1 → 2π.
621: * g_r(u) = e^{ru}(cos u + r|sin u|) → ∞ as r → ∞, so θ1(r) < u for large r. Hence θ1 → π. ∎
622: 
623: *Certified samples (`ctl-e-v1` e1, arb 256 bits, bisection width 2.4·10⁻³³; the signs of g_r − 1 are certified at the
624: rational endpoints).*
625: 
626: | r | θ1(r) | 2π − θ1 | θ1 − π |
627: |---|---|---|---|
628: | 10⁻⁵ | 6.27197544171877006440 | 0.0112099 | |
629: | 10⁻³ | 6.17119870886673768129 | 0.111987 | |
630: | 10⁻² | 5.93199833544080813409 | | |
631: | 0.1 | 5.24260899869797037610 | | |
632: | 0.3 | 4.65987805685937520612 | | |
633: | 1 | 3.94073313569291492508 | | |
634: | 3 | 3.46335292772992627540 | | |
635: | 10 | 3.24126130608095609995 | | |
636: | 100 | 3.15159232027645847667 | | 0.00999967 |
637: 
638: The values are strictly decreasing (certified), and ∂_r g, ∂_u g > 0 hold on every enclosure.
639: 
640: **Lemma 6.6 (moments).** For φ = ωτ: m_k(e^{−t}h_τ) = k!(1 − Re[c ζ^{k+1}]), with c := (1 − ir)e^{(r−i)φ} and
641: ζ := 1/(1 + δ − iω) = 1/(1 + rω − iω). δ = 0 recovers Lemma 5.2.
642: *Proof.* h_τ(t) = 1 − Re[(1 − ir)e^{(δ−iω)τ}e^{(−δ+iω)t}], ∫_0^∞ t^k e^{−(1+δ−iω)t} dt = k!ζ^{k+1}, and
643: e^{(δ−iω)τ} = e^{(r−i)φ}. ∎
644: (`ctl-e-v1` e4: the arb closed form and 40-digit quadrature agree to 4.6·10⁻⁴¹ on a feasible ray.)
645: 
646: **Remark 6.7 (geometry; not used).** K ∩ {a = 1} is the set {v : ⟨−v, z⟩ ≤ 1 ∀ z ∈ Z̄}, where
647: Z̄ := {e^{−δt}(cos ωt, sin ωt) : t ≥ 0} ∪ {0}. This is minus the polar of conv(Z̄); K is the epigraph cone of the
648: support function. ∂conv(Z̄) consists of the spiral arc t ∈ [0, τ1], τ1 := θ1(r)/ω, followed by the segment from z(τ1)
649: back to z(0). The double zero at τ is the touching point of the supporting line, i.e. the maximizer of ⟨−v, z(t)⟩.
650: Supporting lines through the corner z(0) that are not tangent to either edge give the non-extreme case (b).
651: 
652: **Controls (`ctl-e-v1`, 24/24).**
653: * Infeasible rays are detected as negative somewhere:
654:   * at r = 3/10, ω = 1, φ = θ1 + 1/100: h(0) = −0.0441279542429187 ± 1.3·10⁻¹⁷;
655:   * at τ = 2π + 1/10: h(1/10) = 1 − e^{0.6π} = −5.58606196269472;
656:   * at r = 1, ω = 2, φ = θ1(1) + 10⁻³: h(0) = −0.07384.
657: * Feasible ray at φ = θ1 − 1/100: h(0) = 0.0439727 > 0, and h > 0 is certified at every grid point j/64,
658:   j = 0…2560 (t ∈ [0, 40]). The minimum, 2.21·10⁻⁵, is at t = 298/64, next to the double zero τ ≈ 4.65. The
659:   all-t statement is Lemma 6.4, not the grid.
660: * The phases φ ∈ {0, 1.57, 3, 4} at r = 0.3 give h(0) = 0 (exact; the double zero at 0) or h(0) > 0.
661: * Mutations: the enclosure shifted by +1/100 is rejected (g(lo') − 1 = +0.0441 > 0), and the claim "θ1 + 1/100 is
662:   feasible" is rejected (h(0) < 0).
663: 
664: ---
665: 
666: ## 7. What these lemmas reduce the problem to (for the integration owner)
667: 
668: **Corollary 7.1 (exact reduction).** Put
669:   S_2(r, ω, φ) := SCV(e^{−t}h_{φ/ω}) with δ = rω, computed by Lemma 6.6,
670:   I_2 := inf{S_2(r, ω, φ) : r > 0, ω > 0, 0 ≤ φ ≤ θ1(r)}.
671: Then:
672: 1. inf{SCV(X) : X ∈ ME(≤3) continuous} = min(Δ3, v_cos, I_2) = min(v_cos, I_2).
673: 2. **I_2 ≤ v_cos.** Hence the infimum over ME(≤3) equals I_2.
674: 3. The conjecture "SCV ≥ v_cos on ME(≤3)" is equivalent to I_2 ≥ v_cos, i.e. to I_2 = v_cos.
675: 
676: *Proof.* (1) is 2.6, 3.5, L3, 5.1, 6.3 and 6.4, with Δ3 > 553/2000 > 773/3844 ≥ v_cos (Lemma 4.2 and Theorem 5.6;
677: EXACT). For (2), take a D1 minimizer
678: (ω*, φ*) with φ* reduced to [0, 2π). By 6.5, θ1(r) > φ* for all small r > 0, so (r, ω*, φ*) is feasible. The moments
679: of 6.6 are continuous in r at r = 0 with value 5.2, so S_2(r, ω*, φ*) → v_cos. ∎
680: 
681: **Status of the parts.** L1–L6 are proved above in full: written proofs, in which every number on the proof path is
682: EXACT and re-checked by `exact-certs-v1`. The D2 lower bound I_2 ≥ v_cos is *not* addressed here.
683: 
684: **Constants for the D2 work:**
685: * EXACT:
686:   * Δ3 > 553/2000, and Δ3 ∈ (478316921303906167/1729382256910270464, 1275511790143749779/4611686018427387904), i.e.
687:     0.276582530780945666…;
688:   * v_cos ≤ 773/3844 < 1007/5000 < 11/50 = T.
689: * EXACT (written lemmas):
690:   * the D1 uniform bound 1 + SCV ≥ (1 + Δ3)/(1 + ω²);
691:   * the D1 tails: SCV ≥ 428/2125 > 0.2014 for ω ≤ 1/4, and SCV > 0.33 for ω ≥ 4.
692: * COMPUTATIONAL (conditional):
693:   * v_cos < U = 0.20090156350055695880138068245616 (Arb, `ctl-c-v2`);
694:   * the θ1 table (Arb, `ctl-e-v1`; informational);
695:   * the optional lower bound v_cos ≥ 0.20090156350055 (Theorem 5.8, `ctl-f-main-v1`).
696: 
697: **What the theorem uses.** It uses only the written lemmas and the EXACT facts above. In particular it uses neither U
698: nor the optional lower bound (Theorem 5.8).
